## I Mainly About Fenn / 第一章 芬恩其人

===Original===
“When we get licked tomorrow by half-a-dozen wickets,” said Jimmy Silver, tilting his chair until the back touched the wall, “don’t say I didn’t warn you. If you fellows take down what I say from time to time in notebooks, as you ought to do, you’ll remember that I offered to give anyone odds that Kay’s would out us in the final. I always said that a really hot man like Fenn was more good to a side than half-a-dozen ordinary men. He can do all the bowling and all the batting. All the fielding, too, in the slips.”

Tea was just over at Blackburn’s, and the bulk of the house had gone across to preparation in the school buildings. The prefects, as was their custom, lingered on to finish the meal at their leisure. These after-tea conversations were quite an institution at Blackburn’s. The labours of the day were over, and the time for preparation for the morrow had not yet come. It would be time to be thinking of that in another hour. Meanwhile, a little relaxation might be enjoyed. Especially so as this was the last day but two of the summer term, and all necessity for working after tea had ceased with the arrival of the last lap of the examinations.

===Chinese===
「等明天咱们让人家只用六个三柱门就收拾了，」吉米·西尔弗（Jimmy Silver）说着，把椅子往后一仰，椅背抵住墙壁，「可别说我没提醒过你们。你们几位要是照本分把我平时的话一条条记在小本子上，就会记得我早就说过：决赛里凯氏宿舍（Kay’s）准能赢我们，谁不服，我可以奉陪赌一把。我一向就说，像芬恩（Fenn）这样的真高手，一个人对球队的用处顶得上六个平庸之辈。投球他一个人全包，击球也全包。连滑位区的守场，他都能一并代劳。」

布莱克本宿舍（Blackburn’s）的茶点刚刚散场，宿舍里的大队人马已经过到校舍那边的晚自习去了。级长们照老规矩留在后面，慢条斯理把这顿茶吃到底。这种茶后闲谈在布莱克本宿舍算得上一项制度。一天的功课已经完毕，预备明天功课的时辰却还没到，再过一个钟头再操心那个也不迟。眼下呢，不妨先松快松快。尤其今天已是夏季学期的倒数第三天，考试既然进入了最后一程，茶后再用功的必要也就一扫而空了。

===Original===
Silver was head of the house, and captain of its cricket team, which was nearing the end of its last match, the final for the inter-house cup, and﻿—on paper﻿—getting decidedly the worst of it. After riding in triumph over the School House, Bedell’s, and Mulholland’s, Blackburn’s had met its next door neighbour, Kay’s, in the final, and, to the surprise of the great majority of the school, was showing up badly. The match was affording one more example of how a team of average merit all through may sometimes fall before a one-man side. Blackburn’s had the three last men on the list of the first eleven, Silver, Kennedy, and Challis, and at least nine of its representatives had the reputation of being able to knock up a useful twenty or thirty at any time. Kay’s, on the other hand, had one man, Fenn. After him the tail started. But Fenn was such an exceptional all-round man that, as Silver had said, he was as good as half-a-dozen of the Blackburn’s team, equally formidable whether batting or bowling﻿—he headed the school averages at both. He was one of those batsmen who seem to know exactly what sort of ball you are going to bowl before it leaves your hand, and he could hit like another Jessop. As for his bowling, he bowled left hand﻿—always a puzzling eccentricity to an undeveloped batsman﻿—and could send them down very fast or very slow, as he thought best, and it was hard to see which particular brand he was going to serve up before it was actually in midair.

But it is not necessary to enlarge on his abilities. The figures against his name in *Wisden* prove a good deal. The fact that he had steered Kay’s through into the last round of the house-matches proves still more. It was perfectly obvious to everyone that, if only you could get Fenn out for under ten, Kay’s total for that innings would be nearer twenty than forty. They were an appalling side. But then no house bowler had as yet succeeded in getting Fenn out for under ten. In the six innings he had played in the competition up to date, he had made four centuries, an eighty, and a seventy.

===Chinese===
西尔弗是本宿舍的舍长，兼本宿舍板球队的队长。球队正打完最后一场比赛——宿舍杯的决赛——而且照纸面上看，输得相当难看。布莱克本宿舍一路高奏凯歌，先后踩过学校宿舍（School House）、比德尔宿舍（Bedell’s）和马尔霍兰宿舍（Mulholland’s），在决赛里遇上了紧隔壁的凯氏宿舍，而且——让全校绝大多数人大跌眼镜的是——打得糟糕透顶。这场比赛又一次提供了例证：一支从头到尾都中不溜的球队，有时候会栽在一支一人球队手里。布莱克本这边坐拥校队名单上末尾的三员——西尔弗、肯尼迪（Kennedy）和查利斯（Challis），队里至少有九个人素有名声，随时能敲出个顶用的二十分三十分。凯氏那边呢，只有一个人：芬恩。他往后就是长尾巴了。可是芬恩这样百年不遇的全才，正如西尔弗所说，顶得上布莱克本队里六个人：击球也好，投球也好，同样叫人胆寒——这两项的全校平均分，都归他坐头把交椅。他是那种击球手：你的球还没离手，他仿佛已经知道你要投什么路数；而且他抡起棒来，活像又一个杰索普（Jessop）。至于投球，他投左手——对没长成的击球手，这永远是种叫人头疼的怪招——并且要快就快，要慢就慢，全看他觉得怎么最合适；等球真正到了半空，你才看得出他这回端上桌的究竟是哪一路货色。

不过他的本事也毋庸多费笔墨。《威斯登》（*Wisden*）上他名下那一串数字就很能说明问题；他独自领着凯氏宿舍闯进宿舍赛最后一轮的事实，说明的问题更多。谁都看得明明白白：只要能把芬恩十分以内打发掉，凯氏那一局的总分就离二十近、离四十远。他们就是这么一支糟得吓人的球队。可是话又说回来，到目前为止，还没有哪个宿舍的投球手成功地把芬恩十分以内打发掉过。这次比赛里他至今打过六局，敲出四个百分、一个八十分、一个七十分。

===Original===
Kennedy, the second prefect at Blackburn’s, paused in the act of grappling with the remnant of a pot of jam belonging to some person unknown, to reply to Silver’s remarks.

“We aren’t beaten yet,” he said, in his solid way. Kennedy’s chief characteristics were solidity, and an infinite capacity for taking pains. Nothing seemed to tire or discourage him. He kept pegging away till he arrived. The ordinary person, for instance, would have considered the jam-pot, on which he was then engaged, an empty jam-pot. Kennedy saw that there was still a strawberry (or it may have been a section of a strawberry) at the extreme end, and he meant to have that coy vegetable if he had to squeeze the pot to get at it. To take another instance, all the afternoon of the previous day he had bowled patiently at Fenn while the latter lifted every other ball into space. He had been taken off three times, and at every fresh attack he had plodded on doggedly, until at last, as he had expected, the batsman had misjudged a straight one, and he had bowled him all over his wicket. Kennedy generally managed to get there sooner or later.

“It’s no good chucking the game up simply because we’re in a tight place,” he said, bringing the spoon to the surface at last with the section of strawberry adhering to the end of it. “That sort of thing’s awfully feeble.”

“He calls me feeble!” shouted Jimmy Silver. “By James, I’ve put a man to sleep for less.”

It was one of his amusements to express himself from time to time in a melodramatic fashion, sometimes accompanying his words with suitable gestures. It was on one of these occasions﻿—when he had assumed at a moment’s notice the role of the “Baffled Despot,” in an argument with Kennedy in his study on the subject of the house football team﻿—that he broke what Mr. Blackburn considered a valuable door with a poker. Since then he had moderated his transports.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪——布莱克本宿舍的第二级长——此刻正埋头对付一罐不知哪位人士留下的果酱残骸，闻言停下来答西尔弗的话。

「我们还没输呢，」他以他那扎实的口气说道。肯尼迪的主要特色就是扎实，外加无穷无尽的肯下苦功。似乎什么也不能叫他疲倦或者灰心。他就是这样不声不响地一寸寸磨，磨到抵达目的地为止。打个比方，换了寻常人，早把他手里那罐果酱当作空罐子扔掉了。肯尼迪却看出罐子最深处还有一粒草莓（也可能只是半粒），而且他打定了主意，哪怕把罐子挤扁，也要把那枚羞答答的果蔬弄到手。再举一例：前一天整整一个下午，他任劳任怨地朝芬恩投球，而芬恩隔一个球就把球送上青天。他三次被撤下投球，每次重新上阵，都倔头倔脑地一球一球往前磨，直到最后，正如他所料，击球手看错了一记直线球，被他结结实实投了个正着。肯尼迪总有本事早晚到达目的地。

「不能因为眼下处境不妙，就把比赛一扔了事，」他说。勺子终于捞出了水面，勺尖上黏着那半粒草莓。「那种做派可太没出息了。」

「他居然好意思说我没出息！」吉米·西尔弗嚷道，「老天在上，为比这还轻的事，我都把人打发睡过觉。」

他的消遣之一，就是时不时用一派闹剧腔发表言论，有时还配上相称的手势。有那么一回——他在自己书房里同肯尼迪争论宿舍橄榄球队的问题，当场一秒入戏，给自己派上「受挫的暴君」一角——他用一根拨火棍砸破了布莱克本先生（Mr. Blackburn）认为相当值钱的一扇门。自那以后，他的万丈豪情多少收敛了些。

===Original===
“They’ve got to make seventy-nine,” said Kennedy.

Challis, the other first eleven man, was reading a green scoring-book.

“I don’t think Kay’s ought to have the face to stick the cup up in their dining-room,” he said, “considering the little they’ve done to win it. If they *do* win it, that is. Still, as they made two hundred first innings, they ought to be able to knock off seventy-nine. But I was saying that the pot ought to go to Fenn. Lot the rest of the team had to do with it. Blackburn’s, first innings, hundred and fifty-one; Fenn, eight for forty-nine. Kay’s, two hundred and one; Fenn, a hundred and sixty-four not out. Second innings, Blackburn’s hundred and twenty-eight; Fenn ten for eighty. Bit thick, isn’t it? I suppose that’s what you’d call a one-man team.”

===Chinese===
「他们还得拿七十九分呢。」肯尼迪说。

查利斯——另一位校队选手——正在翻一本绿皮记分簿。

「我看凯氏宿舍还真好意思把奖杯供进他们饭厅，」他说，「想想他们为赢它出了多大力气。当然，前提是他们真能赢下来。不过，他们首局既然拿了两百，敲下七十九分应该不成问题。可我刚才想说的是，这奖杯干脆发给芬恩一个人得了。队里其他人沾了什么光？布莱克本首局一百五十一分，芬恩四十九分取八柱。凯氏两百零一分，芬恩不出局一百六十四分。次局布莱克本一百二十八分，芬恩八十分取十柱。够狠的吧？我想这就是人家说的一人球队。」

===Original===
Williams, one of the other prefects, who had just sat down at the piano for the purpose of playing his one tune﻿—a cakewalk, of which, through constant practice, he had mastered the rudiments﻿—spoke over his shoulder to Silver.

“I tell you what, Jimmy,” he said, “you’ve probably lost us the pot by getting your people to send brother Billy to Kay’s. If he hadn’t kept up his wicket yesterday, Fenn wouldn’t have made half as many.”

When his young brother had been sent to Eckleton two terms before, Jimmy Silver had strongly urged upon his father the necessity of placing him in some house other than Blackburn’s. He felt that a head of a house, even of so orderly and perfect a house as Blackburn’s, has enough worries without being saddled with a small brother. And on the previous afternoon young Billy Silver, going in eighth wicket for Kay’s, had put a solid bat in front of everything for the space of one hour, in the course of which he made ten runs and Fenn sixty. By scoring odd numbers off the last ball of each over, Fenn had managed to secure the majority of the bowling in the most masterly way.

===Chinese===
威廉斯（Williams）——又一位级长——刚在钢琴前坐定，准备弹他那独一无二的一支曲子：一支步态舞曲，凭着勤学苦练，他已掌握了它的入门部分。他隔着肩膀同西尔弗搭话。

「我说，吉米，」他说，「这奖杯八成就是叫你给弄丢的——谁让你撺掇你家大人把比利兄弟送进凯氏宿舍。昨天要不是他死守住三柱门，芬恩的得分连一半都到不了。」

他这个小弟弟是两个学期前被送进埃克尔顿（Eckleton）的，当时吉米·西尔弗极力向他父亲进言，务必要把弟弟安插在布莱克本以外的随便哪所宿舍。他的道理是：一个舍长，哪怕管的是布莱克本这样规矩完备的宿舍，烦恼也已经够多了，犯不着再背上一个小兄弟。而前一天下午，小比利·西尔弗（Billy Silver）替凯氏第八个三柱门出场，整整一个钟头，把一根坚实的球棒堵在每一记来球面前，其间他自己得了十分，芬恩得了六十分。芬恩靠每一轮最后一球专打单数，以最高明的手段把投球的大头稳稳抓在自己手里。

===Original===
“These things will happen,” said Silver, resignedly. “We Silvers, you know, can’t help making runs. Come on, Williams, let’s have that tune, and get it over.”

Williams obliged. It was a classic piece called “The Coon Band Contest,” remarkable partly for a taking melody, partly for the vast possibilities of noise which it afforded. Williams made up for his failure to do justice to the former by a keen appreciation of the latter. He played the piece through again, in order to correct the mistakes he had made at his first rendering of it. Then he played it for the third time to correct a new batch of errors.

“I should like to hear Fenn play that,” said Challis. “You’re awfully good, you know, Williams, but he might do it better still.”

“Get him to play it as an encore at the concert,” said Williams, starting for the fourth time.

The talented Fenn was also a musician﻿—not a genius at the piano, as he was at cricket, but a sufficiently sound performer for his age, considering that he had not made a special study of it. He was to play at the school concert on the following day.

===Chinese===
「这种事在所难免，」西尔弗听天由命地说，「要知道，我们西尔弗家的人，天生就会得分，拦都拦不住。来吧，威廉斯，把那支曲子放出来，早完早了。」

威廉斯从命了。那是一支题为《黑人乐队大赛》的经典作品，其可观之处，一半在曲调动人，一半在它提供的惊天动地的音量上的种种可能。威廉斯对前者的演绎固然力不从心，对后者的欣赏却格外到位。他把这支曲子从头到尾又弹了一遍，以纠正第一次演奏中犯下的错误；接着又弹了第三遍，以纠正新冒出来的一批错。

「我倒想听听芬恩弹那支曲子，」查利斯说，「你弹得确实好极了，威廉斯，可他兴许还能弹得更好。」

「那就让他在音乐会上当个加演曲目，」威廉斯说着，开始了第四遍。

那位多才多艺的芬恩还是个音乐家——不像打板球那样是钢琴上的天才，但就其年龄而论已算得相当过得去的演奏者，何况他并不曾专门下过功夫。他预定在第二天的学校音乐会上登台演奏。

===Original===
“I believe Fenn has an awful time at Kay’s,” said Jimmy Silver. “It must be a fair sort of hole, judging from the specimens you see crawling about in Kay caps. I wish I’d known my people were sending young Billy there. I’d have warned them. I only told them not to sling him in here. I had no idea they’d have picked Kay’s.”

“Fenn was telling me the other day,” said Kennedy, “that being in Kay’s had spoiled his whole time at the school. He always wanted to come to Blackburn’s, only there wasn’t room that particular term. Bad luck, wasn’t it? I don’t think he found it so bad before he became head of the house. He didn’t come into contact with Kay so much. But now he finds that he can’t do a thing without Kay buzzing round and interfering.”

“I wonder,” said Jimmy Silver, thoughtfully, “if that’s why he bowls so fast. To work it off, you know.”

In the course of a beautiful innings of fifty-three that afternoon, the captain of Blackburn’s had received two of Fenn’s speediest on the same spot just above the pad in rapid succession, and he now hobbled painfully when he moved about.

The conversation that evening had dealt so largely with Fenn﻿—the whole school, indeed, was talking of nothing but his great attempt to win the cricket cup single-handed﻿—that Kennedy, going out into the road for a breather before the rest of the boarders returned from preparation, made his way to Kay’s to see if Fenn was imitating his example, and taking the air too.

===Chinese===
「我看芬恩在凯氏宿舍的日子够呛，」吉米·西尔弗说，「瞧瞧那些戴着凯氏小帽四处乱晃的样品，就知道那准是个不赖的窟窿。早知道我家大人要把小比利往那儿送，我好歹提醒他们一声。我只嘱咐过他们别把他塞到我们这儿来，压根没想到他们会挑中凯氏。」

「芬恩前几天跟我说，」肯尼迪道，「进了凯氏宿舍，把他在学校的整段日子都毁了。他一心想来布莱克本，偏偏那个学期没有空额。够倒霉的，是不是？我看他没当上舍长之前还没觉得这么糟，跟凯先生（Kay）打照面的时候也不多。可如今他发现，自己不管做什么事，凯先生都要在旁边嗡嗡地围着捣乱。」

「我倒纳闷，」吉米·西尔弗若有所思地说，「他投球投得那么快，是不是就为的这个。借机发泄发泄，你懂的。」

那天下午，这位布莱克本队长在他漂亮的五十三分一局里，接连在同一块地方——护腿正上方——挨了芬恩两个最快的球，这会儿他一挪步就疼得龇牙咧嘴。

那天晚上的谈话既然大半围着芬恩打转——事实上，全校上下谈的也无非是他孤身一人争夺板球杯的壮举——于是肯尼迪趁其余寄宿生还没从晚自习回来，先出门到路上透口气，顺路往凯氏宿舍那边去，看看芬恩是不是也在学他的样，出来换换空气。

===Original===
He found him at Kay’s gate, and they strolled towards the school buildings together. Fenn was unusually silent.

“Well?” said Kennedy, after a minute had passed without a remark.

“Well, what?”

“What’s up?”

Fenn laughed what novelists are fond of calling a mirthless laugh.

“Oh, I don’t know,” he said; “I’m sick of this place.”

===Chinese===
他在凯氏大门口撞见了芬恩，两人便一道朝校舍踱去。芬恩反常地沉默着。

「怎么样？」等了一分钟没等到一句话，肯尼迪开了口。

「怎么样，什么？」

「出什么事了？」

芬恩发出了一声小说家们最爱称作「无笑之笑」的那种笑。

「哦，说不上来，」他说，「我是待腻这个地方了。」

===Original===
Kennedy inspected his friend’s face anxiously by the light of the lamp over the school gate. There was no mistake about it. Fenn certainly did look bad. His face always looked lean and craggy, but tonight there was a difference. He looked used up.

“Fagged?” asked Kennedy.

“No. Sick.”

“What about?”

“Everything. I wish you could come into Kay’s for a bit just to see what it’s like. Then you’d understand. At present I don’t suppose you’ve an idea of it. I’d like to write a book on ‘Kay Day by Day.’ I’d have plenty to put in it.”

“What’s he been doing?”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪借着校门上方那盏灯，忧心忡忡地端详朋友的脸。错不了，芬恩看上去确实糟糕。他的脸本来就又瘦又多棱角，今晚却又不一样，一副被榨干了的模样。

「累的？」肯尼迪问。

「不是，是腻的。」

「腻什么？」

「什么都腻。你要是能进凯氏待上几天，亲眼看看那是什么光景就好了。那样你才能明白。眼下我估摸你对此毫无概念。我很想写一本书，就叫《凯氏日常》，有的是材料往里搁。」

「他干什么好事了？」

===Original===
“Oh, nothing out of the ordinary run. It’s the fact that he’s always at it that does me. You get a houseful of﻿—well, you know the sort of chap the average Kayite is. They’d keep me busy even if I were allowed a free hand. But I’m not. Whenever I try and keep order and stop things a bit, out springs the man Kay from nowhere, and takes the job out of my hands, makes a ghastly mess of everything, and retires purring. Once in every three times, or thereabouts, he slangs me in front of the kids for not keeping order. I’m glad this is the end of the term. I couldn’t stand it much longer. Hullo, here come the chaps from prep. We’d better be getting back.”

===Chinese===
「哦，没什么出格的。烦就烦在他无时无刻不在忙活。你收进一屋子——唉，寻常的凯氏人是什么材料，你也知道。就算放我放开手脚去管，他们也够我忙的。可我偏偏连这个都没有。每回我想维持维持秩序、稍稍约束一下，凯先生就不知打哪儿蹦出来，把差事从我手里抢过去，把一切搅得一塌糊涂，然后心满意足地打着呼噜退场。大约每三回里就有一回，他当着那帮小的面骂我不维持秩序。幸亏这学期到头了，再拖下去我可撑不住。喂，上晚自习的回来了。咱们还是回去吧。」

## II An Evening at Kay’s / 第二章 凯氏宿舍的一晚

===Original===
They turned, and began to walk towards the houses. Kennedy felt miserable. He never allowed himself to be put out, to any great extent, by his own worries, which, indeed, had not been very numerous up to the present, but the misfortunes of his friends always troubled him exceedingly. When anything happened to him personally, he found the discomfort of being in a tight place largely counterbalanced by the excitement of trying to find a way out. But the impossibility of helping Fenn in any way depressed him.

“It must be awful,” he said, breaking the silence.

“It is,” said Fenn, briefly.

===Chinese===
两人转身朝各宿舍走去。肯尼迪心里很不好受。他自己烦心的事从不至于叫他太过沮丧——事实上他至今也没遇到过几桩——可朋友的不幸却总是叫他格外揪心。他自己遇上什么事的时候，身处窘境的不痛快，会被寻找出路的兴奋冲抵掉大半。可是这回他无论如何帮不上芬恩的忙，这叫他闷闷不乐。

「那一定很不好受。」他打破沉默说。

「可不是。」芬恩简短地说。

===Original===
“But haven’t the house-matches made any difference? Blackburn’s always frightfully bucked when the house does anything. You can do anything you like with him if you lift a cup. I should have thought Kay would have been all right when he saw you knocking up centuries, and getting into the final, and all that sort of thing.”

Fenn laughed.

“Kay!” he said. “My dear man, he doesn’t *know*. I don’t suppose he’s got the remotest idea that we are in the final at all, or, if he has, he doesn’t understand what being in the final means.”

===Chinese===
「可是宿舍赛就一点没让情况改观吗？宿舍一有出息，布莱克本先生总要乐开花。只要你能捧回一只奖杯，让他干什么都行。我本想着，凯先生眼看你一记记百分、一路打进决赛什么的，总该顺气了吧。」

芬恩笑了。

「凯先生！」他说，「老兄，他压根*不知道*。我看他八成连我们进了决赛这件事都毫无概念；就算有，他也不明白进决赛是怎么回事。」

===Original===
“But surely he’ll be glad if you lick us tomorrow?” asked Kennedy. Such indifference on the part of a housemaster respecting the fortunes of his house seemed to him, having before him the bright example of Mr. Blackburn, almost incredible.

“I don’t suppose so,” said Fenn. “Or, if he is, I’ll bet he doesn’t show it. He’s not like Blackburn. I wish he was. Here he comes, so perhaps we’d better talk about something else.”

The vanguard of the boys returning from preparation had passed them, and they were now standing at the gate of the house. As Fenn spoke, a little, restless-looking man in cap and gown came up. His clean-shaven face wore an expression of extreme alertness﻿—the sort of look a ferret wears as he slips in at the mouth of a rabbit-hole. A doctor, called upon to sum up Mr. Kay at a glance, would probably have said that he suffered from nerves, which would have been a perfectly correct diagnosis, though none of the members of his house put his manners and customs down to that cause. They considered that the methods he pursued in the management of the house were the outcome of a naturally malignant disposition. This was, however, not the case. There is no reason to suppose that Mr. Kay did not mean well. But there is no doubt that he was extremely fussy. And fussiness﻿—with the possible exceptions of homicidal mania and a taste for arson﻿—is quite the worst characteristic it is possible for a housemaster to possess.

===Chinese===
「可要是你们明天赢了咱们，他总该高兴吧？」肯尼迪问。有布莱克本先生那样光辉的榜样摆在眼前，一位舍监对自己宿舍的胜负如此漠不关心，在他看来简直不可思议。

「我看未必，」芬恩说，「就算高兴，我敢打赌他也绝不挂在脸上。他不是布莱克本那样的人。我倒真希望他是。他来了，咱们最好换个话题。」

从晚自习回来的学生们，先头部队已经从他们身边过去，这时两人正站在宿舍大门口。芬恩话音未落，一个戴方帽穿长袍、个头矮小、坐立不安模样的男人走了过来。他那张刮得干干净净的脸上带着一种高度警觉的神情——活像一只雪貂钻进兔洞洞口时的那副表情。假如请一位医生对凯先生看上一眼便下诊断，多半会说他神经紧张。这诊断完全正确，只是他宿舍里的学生谁也不肯把他的做派归因于此。他们认为，他治理宿舍的种种手段，出自一副天生的坏心肠。然而事实并非如此。没有理由认为凯先生不是一片好意。可他确实是个爱瞎操心的人，这一点毫无疑问。而瞎操心——也许除了杀人狂和纵火癖之外——实在是一位舍监可能有的最糟糕的品性了。

===Original===
He caught sight of Fenn and Kennedy at the gate, and stopped in his stride.

“What are you doing here, Fenn?” he asked, with an abruptness which brought a flush to the latter’s face. “Why are you outside the house?”

Kennedy began to understand why it was that his friend felt so strongly on the subject of his housemaster. If this was the sort of thing that happened every day, no wonder that there was dissension in the house of Kay. He tried to imagine Blackburn speaking in that way to Jimmy Silver or himself, but his imagination was unequal to the task. Between Mr. Blackburn and his prefects there existed a perfect understanding. He relied on them to see that order was kept, and they acted accordingly. Fenn, by the exercise of considerable self-control, had always been scrupulously polite to Mr. Kay.

“I came out to get some fresh air before lockup, sir,” he replied.

===Chinese===
他一眼望见门口的芬恩和肯尼迪，脚步一顿。

「你在这儿干什么，芬恩？」他问，语气之突兀，说得后者脸上一红。「你为什么在宿舍外面？」

肯尼迪开始明白，他的朋友为什么一提起自家舍监就那么激动。如果这就是每天都上演的戏码，难怪凯氏宿舍里怨声四起。他试着想象布莱克本先生这样同吉米·西尔弗或者他本人说话，可惜想象力不济。布莱克本先生和他的级长们之间存在着完美的默契：他放手让他们维持秩序，他们也照着办。而芬恩凭着极大的自制力，对凯先生始终彬彬有礼。

「我出来在锁门前透透气，先生。」他答道。

===Original===
“Well, go in. Go in at once. I cannot allow you to be outside the house at this hour. Go indoors directly.”

Kennedy expected a scene, but Fenn took it quite quietly.

“Good night, Kennedy,” he said.

“So long,” said Kennedy.

Fenn caught his eye, and smiled painfully. Then he turned and went into the house.

===Chinese===
「行了，进去。马上进去。我不能容许你这个时辰还在宿舍外面。立刻进屋去。」

肯尼迪本以为要闹一场，芬恩却不动声色地受了。

「晚安，肯尼迪。」他说。

「回头见。」肯尼迪说。

芬恩同他对了下眼神，苦笑了一下，随即转身进了宿舍。

===Original===
Mr. Kay’s zeal for reform was apparently still unsatisfied. He directed his batteries towards Kennedy.

“Go to your house at once, Kennedy. You have no business out here at this time.”

This, thought Kennedy, was getting a bit too warm. Mr. Kay might do as he pleased with his own house, but he was hanged if he was going to trample on *him*.

“Mr. Blackburn is my housemaster, sir,” he said with great respect.

===Chinese===
凯先生的整肃热望显然还没得到满足。他又把炮口对准了肯尼迪。

「马上回你自己宿舍去，肯尼迪。这个时辰你无权待在外面。」

肯尼迪心想，这可就有点过火了。凯先生在他自己宿舍里爱怎么折腾随他，可要是想踩到*他*头上来，那是休想。

「布莱克本先生是我的舍监，先生。」他毕恭毕敬地说。

===Original===
Mr. Kay stared.

“My housemaster,” continued Kennedy with gusto, slightly emphasising the first word, “knows that I always go out just before lockup, and he has no objection.”

And, to emphasise this point, he walked towards the school buildings again. For a moment it seemed as if Mr. Kay intended to call him back, but he thought better of it. Mr. Blackburn, in normal circumstances a pacific man, had one touchy point﻿—his house. He resented any interference with its management, and was in the habit of saying so. Mr. Kay remembered one painful scene in the Masters’ Common Room, when he had ventured to let fall a few well-meant hints as to how a house should be ruled. Really, he had thought Blackburn would have choked. Better, perhaps, to leave him to look after his own affairs.

So Mr. Kay followed Fenn indoors, and Kennedy, having watched him vanish, made his way to Blackburn’s.

===Chinese===
凯先生愣住了。

「我的舍监，」肯尼迪津津有味地接着说，头一个词稍微加了点重音，「知道我一向在锁门前出去走走，他没有意见。」

为了加重这句话的分量，他又朝校舍那边走去。有一瞬间，凯先生似乎想把他叫回来，但转念作罢。布莱克本先生平日是个和气人，只有一处逆鳞——他的宿舍。谁要是插手宿舍的管理，他都要记恨，而且习惯把这话明说。凯先生想起教师公共教研室里那不堪回首的一幕：当时他斗胆就宿舍治理之道垂示了几句好心的意见。说真的，他当时都以为布莱克本要背过气去。还是随他自己照管自家的事务为妙。

于是凯先生跟着芬恩进了屋；肯尼迪目送他消失，便回布莱克本宿舍去了。

===Original===
Quietly as Fenn had taken the incident at the gate, it nevertheless rankled. He read prayers that night in a distinctly unprayerful mood. It seemed to him that it would be lucky if he could get through to the end of the term before Mr. Kay applied that last straw which does not break the backs of camels only. Eight weeks’ holiday, with plenty of cricket, would brace him up for another term. And he had been invited to play for the county against Middlesex four days after the holidays began. That should have been a soothing thought. But it really seemed to make matters worse. It was hard that a man who on Monday would be bowling against Warner and Beldam, or standing up to Trott and Hearne, should on the preceding Tuesday be sent indoors like a naughty child by a man who stood five-feet-one in his boots, and was devoid of any sort of merit whatever.

It seemed to him that it would help him to sleep peacefully that night if he worked off a little of his just indignation upon somebody. There was a noise going on in the fags’ room. There always was at Kay’s. It was not a particularly noisy noise﻿—considering; but it had better be stopped. Badly as Kay had treated him, he remembered that he was head of the house, and as such it behoved him to keep order in the house.

===Chinese===
门口那一场，芬恩虽然不动声色地受了，心里却憋着一股火。当晚他领晚祷时，那心情分明毫无祷告的意味。在他看来，能不能撑到学期结束、不叫凯先生压上那根最后稻草——而压垮的又岂止骆驼的脊背——都得算是运气。八个礼拜的假期，加上充足的板球，会给他打起精神再熬一学期的气力。而且假期开始四天后，他已受邀代表郡队出战米德尔塞克斯（Middlesex）。这本该是一帖安神的良方，谁知反倒像是火上浇油。一个人礼拜一就要同沃纳（Warner）和贝尔达姆（Beldam）较量投球，或者直面特罗特（Trott）和赫恩（Hearne）的击球，头一个礼拜二却像个淘气孩子似的，被一个穿上靴子才五英尺一寸高、身上找不出半分可取之处的人赶进屋里去——这口气实在难咽。

他觉得，当晚要想睡个安稳觉，最好先找个人把这一腔义愤发泄掉一点。跑腿生（fag）室里正闹得响。凯氏宿舍从来如此。平心而论，那喧闹声也并不算特别响——可还是制止为宜。凯先生再怎么亏待他，他也还记得自己是舍长，既有此身份，维持宿舍秩序便是分内之事。

===Original===
He went downstairs, and, on arriving on the scene of action, found that the fags were engaged upon spirited festivities, partly in honour of the near approach of the summer holidays, partly because﻿—miracles barred﻿—the house was going on the morrow to lift the cricket-cup. There were a good many books flying about, and not a few slippers. There was a confused mass rolling in combat on the floor, and the table was occupied by a scarlet-faced individual, who passed the time by kicking violently at certain hands, which were endeavouring to drag him from his post, and shrieking frenzied abuse at the owners of the said hands. It was an animated scene, and to a deaf man might have been most enjoyable.

Fenn’s appearance was the signal for a temporary suspension of hostilities.

“What the dickens is all this row about?” he inquired.

===Chinese===
他下了楼，一到事发现场，便发现跑腿生们正投身于热火朝天的庆祝：一半是为了致敬近在眼前的暑假，一半是因为——除非出现奇迹——本宿舍明天就要捧起板球杯。满屋子飞着不少书，拖鞋也飞了不少。地板上有一团人影纠缠着滚作一团，桌上则盘踞着一位满面通红的人物，正用脚猛踹几只试图把他从岗位上拽下来的手，同时朝那几只手的主人尖声叫骂，用语不堪入耳。场面着实热闹，落在聋子耳里没准还是一大享受。

芬恩一露面，交战双方便暂时休了兵。

「这么吵吵闹闹的，究竟是怎么回事？」他问道。

===Original===
No one seemed ready at the moment with a concise explanation. There was an awkward silence. One or two of the weaker spirits even went so far as to sit down and begin to read. All would have been well but for a bright idea which struck some undiscovered youth at the back of the room.

“Three cheers for Fenn!” observed this genial spirit, in no uncertain voice.

The idea caught on. It was just what was wanted to give a finish to the evening’s festivities. Fenn had done well by the house. He had scored four centuries and an eighty, and was going to knock off the runs against Blackburn’s tomorrow off his own bat. Also, he had taken eighteen wickets in the final house-match. Obviously Fenn was a person deserving of all encouragement. It would be a pity to let him think that his effort had passed unnoticed by the fags’ room. Happy thought! Three cheers and one more, and then “He’s a jolly good fellow,” to wind up with.

===Chinese===
一时似乎谁也拿不出一段简洁的解释。场面上尴尬地静了下来。一两个胆子小的甚至索性坐下来，装模作样读起了书。要不是屋后头哪个无名少年灵机一动，本可以就此太平。

「为芬恩欢呼三声！」这位热心肠用毫不含糊的嗓音提议道。

这主意立刻大受欢迎。正好给今晚的盛会收个尾。芬恩为宿舍立了大功：他敲了四个百分加一个八十分，明天还要凭一己之力敲掉布莱克本的得分；再说，宿舍杯决赛里他一个人取了十八柱。显而易见，芬恩是个值得一切鼓励的人。要是让他以为跑腿生室对他的功劳无动于衷，岂不可惜。好主意！欢呼三声，再加一声，最后来一首《他是个大好人》，圆满收场。

===Original===
It was while those familiar words, “It’s a way we have in the public scho-o-o-o-ls,” were echoing through the room in various keys, that a small and energetic form brushed past Fenn as he stood in the doorway, vainly trying to stop the fags’ choral efforts.

It was Mr. Kay.

The singing ceased gradually, very gradually. It was some time before Mr. Kay could make himself heard. But after a couple of minutes there was a lull, and the housemaster’s address began to be audible.

“… unendurable noise. What is the meaning of it? I will not have it. Do you hear? It is disgraceful. Every boy in this room will write me two hundred lines by tomorrow evening. It is abominable, Fenn.” He wheeled round towards the head of the house. “Fenn, I am surprised at you standing here and allowing such a disgraceful disturbance to go on. Really, if you cannot keep order better﻿—It is disgraceful, disgraceful.”

===Chinese===
正当那句大家耳熟能详的歌词——「这——就是咱们公——学——学——校——的老规矩」——在各路人马的调门里回荡之际，一个小巧而精力充沛的身影，从站在门口徒劳地试图制止这场大合唱的芬恩身边一擦而过。

来者正是凯先生。

歌声渐渐停了，停得非常之慢。过了好一阵子，凯先生才让别人听见他说话。不过两三分钟后，屋里总算静了下来，舍监的训话开始清晰可闻。

「……不堪忍受的喧闹。这是什么意思？我绝不允许。听见没有？太不成体统了。这屋里每个学生，明天晚上以前给我抄两百行罚抄行。真是岂有此理，芬恩。」他倏地转向舍长，「芬恩，你就站在这儿，任凭这样丢人的骚乱继续下去，我真对你感到失望。说真的，你要是连秩序都维持得不能更好一点——太不像话了，太不像话了。」

===Original===
Mr. Kay shot out of the room. Fenn followed in his wake, and the procession made its way to the housemasters’ study. It had been a near thing, but the last straw had arrived before the holidays.

Mr. Kay wheeled round as he reached his study door.

“Well, Fenn?”

Fenn said nothing.

“Have you anything you wish to say, Fenn?”

“I thought you might have something to say to me, sir.”

===Chinese===
凯先生一阵风似的冲出屋子。芬恩跟在他身后，这一行两人径直来到舍监书房。好险，到底还是没等到放假，最后那根稻草先到了。

凯先生走到书房门口，猛地回过身来。

「怎么样，芬恩？」

芬恩一言不发。

「你有什么话要说吗，芬恩？」

「我倒以为，先生您也许有话要对我说。」

===Original===
“I do not understand you, Fenn.”

“I thought you might wish to apologise for slanging me in front of the fags.”

It is wonderful what a difference the last straw will make in one’s demeanour to a person.

“Apologise! I think you forget whom it is you are speaking to.”

When a master makes this well-worn remark, the wise youth realises that the time has come to close the conversation. All Fenn’s prudence, however, had gone to the four winds.

“If you wanted to tell me I was not fit to be head of the house, you needn’t have done it before a roomful of fags. How do you think I can keep order in the house if you do that sort of thing?”

===Chinese===
「我不明白你的意思，芬恩。」

「我以为，先生您也许愿意为当着一屋子跑腿生骂我赔个不是。」

最后那根稻草能叫一个人对另一个人的态度起多大的变化，实在令人惊叹。

「赔不是！我看你是忘了自己在同谁说话。」

老师说出这句用旧了的老话时，聪明的少年便知道，收起话头的时候到了。然而芬恩的种种谨慎早已飞到九霄云外。

「您要是想告诉我，我不配当这个舍长，犯不着当着一屋子跑腿生的面说。您想想，您要是老来这么一手，我还怎么维持宿舍的秩序？」

===Original===
Mr. Kay overcame his impulse to end the interview abruptly in order to put in a thrust.

“You do not keep order in the house, Fenn,” he said, acidly.

“I do when I am not interfered with.”

“You will be good enough to say ‘sir’ when you speak to me, Fenn,” said Mr. Kay, thereby scoring another point. In the stress of the moment, Fenn had not noticed the omission.

He was silenced. And before he could recover himself, Mr. Kay was in his study, and there was a closed, forbidding door between them.

And as he stared at it, it began slowly to dawn upon Fenn that he had not shown up to advantage in the recent interview. In a word, he had made a fool of himself.

===Chinese===
凯先生按捺住当场结束谈话的冲动，好补上一记冷枪。

「你根本没有维持宿舍的秩序，芬恩。」他尖刻地说。

「只要没人从中作梗，我维持得很好。」

「跟我说话的时候，请你称『先生』，芬恩。」凯先生说道，又赢了一分。方才情急之下，芬恩没留意自己漏了称呼。

他哑口无言。还没等他缓过神来，凯先生已经进了书房，两人之间隔上了一扇关着的、冷冰冰的门。

他盯着那扇门，渐渐回过味来：刚才那场交锋，自己实在没占着什么上风。一句话，他出了洋相。

## III The Final House-Match / 第三章 宿舍杯决赛

===Original===
Blackburn’s took the field at three punctually on the following afternoon, to play out the last act of the final house-match. They were not without some small hope of victory, for curious things happen at cricket, especially in the fourth innings of a match. And runs are admitted to be easier saved than made. Yet seventy-nine seemed an absurdly small score to try and dismiss a team for, and in view of the fact that that team contained a batsman like Fenn, it seemed smaller still. But Jimmy Silver, resolutely as he had declared victory impossible to his intimate friends, was not the man to depress his team by letting it become generally known that he considered Blackburn’s chances small.

===Chinese===
第二天下午三点整，布莱克本宿舍列队登场，来演完宿舍杯决赛的最后一幕。他们并非全无一点获胜的小小指望，因为板球场上怪事常有，尤其在一场比赛的第四局里。而且行家公认，守分总比取分容易。话虽如此，想凭七十九分就把一支球队打发掉，未免小得离谱；再想到那支队里还有芬恩这样的击球手，就更显得小了。不过，吉米·西尔弗虽然在挚友面前一口咬定胜利无望，却绝不是那种会把自己看衰的心思嚷嚷得全队皆知、好叫大家泄气的人。

===Original===
“You must work like niggers in the field,” he said; “don’t give away a run. Seventy-nine isn’t much to make, but if we get Fenn out for a few, they won’t come near it.”

He did not add that in his opinion Fenn would take very good care that he did not get out for a few. It was far more likely that he would make that seventy-nine off his own bat in a dozen overs.

“You’d better begin, Kennedy,” he continued, “from the top end. Place your men where you want ’em. I should have an extra man in the deep, if I were you. That’s where Fenn kept putting them last innings. And you’ll want a short leg, only for goodness sake keep them off the leg-side if you can. It’s a safe four to Fenn every time if you don’t. Look out, you chaps. Man in.”

Kay’s first pair were coming down the pavilion steps.

===Chinese===
「你们在场上得像黑奴一样卖命，」他说，「一分也别白送。七十九分不算多，可只要咱们早早把芬恩打发掉，他们连边都摸不着。」

他没有补上一句：依他看，芬恩会万分小心，决不至于早早出局。多半还是芬恩自己在一来二去十几轮里，凭一己之力敲掉那七十九分。

「你先投吧，肯尼迪，」他接着说，「从上端投起。人手随你摆布。换了我，就在外野深处加派一个人。上一局芬恩净往那儿送球。近腿位也得设一个，只是千万看在老天爷份上，能不往腿侧喂球就别喂。不然芬恩回回稳拿四分。都留神，各位。击球员上场。」

凯氏的开局二人正走下看台的台阶。

===Original===
Challis, going to his place at short slip, called Silver’s attention to a remarkable fact.

“Hullo,” he said, “why isn’t Fenn coming in first?”

“What! By Jove, nor he is. That’s queer. All the better for us. You might get a bit finer, Challis, in case they snick ’em.”

Wayburn, who had accompanied Fenn to the wicket at the beginning of Kay’s first innings, had now for his partner one Walton, a large, unpleasant-looking youth, said to be a bit of a bruiser, and known to be a black sheep. He was one of those who made life at Kay’s so close an imitation of an Inferno. His cricket was of a rustic order. He hit hard and high. When allowed to do so, he hit often. But, as a rule, he left early, a prey to the slips or deep fields. Today was no exception to that rule.

===Chinese===
查利斯走向他近滑位的位置时，唤起了西尔弗对一个反常事实的注意。

「喂，」他说，「芬恩怎么不第一个上场？」

「什么！老天，还真是。这就怪了。对咱们只更有利。查利斯，你不妨站得再贴近一点，防着他们擦棒。」

韦伯恩（Wayburn）在凯氏首局开局时曾陪芬恩一同走向三柱门，如今他的搭档换成了沃尔顿（Walton）——一个块头很大、面目不善的少年，据说打架有两下子，出了名的问题人物。凯氏宿舍之所以那么像一座但丁的地狱，他堪称功臣之一。他的板球是庄稼把式：打得又狠又高；只要没人拦着，就打得起劲。可十有八九，他早早便退场了，不是栽在滑位区手里，就是栽在外野深处手里。今天也逃不出这条规律。

===Original===
Kennedy’s first ball was straight and medium-paced. It was a little too short, however, and Walton, letting go at it with a semicircular sweep like the drive of a golfer, sent it soaring over mid-on’s head and over the boundary. Cheers from the pavilion.

Kennedy bowled his second ball with the same purposeful air, and Walton swept at it as before. There was a click, and Jimmy Silver, who was keeping wicket, took the ball comfortably on a level with his chin.

“How’s that?”

The umpire’s hand went up, and Walton went out﻿—reluctantly, murmuring legends of how he had not gone within a yard of the thing.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪第一球又直又匀速。只是稍短了些，沃尔顿抡出一记高尔夫挥杆式的半圆大扫击，球腾空而起，越过投球侧内场手头顶，飞出了边界。看台上一片欢呼。

肯尼迪以同样胸有成竹的神气投出第二球，沃尔顿照旧大扫过去。只听嗒的一响，守三柱门的吉米·西尔弗在齐下巴的高度稳稳把球接住。

「怎么样？」

裁判的手举了起来，沃尔顿退场了——一百个不情愿，嘴里还念念有词，坚称自己离那球足足有一码远。

===Original===
It was only when the next batsman who emerged from the pavilion turned out to be his young brother and not Fenn, that Silver began to see that something was wrong. It was conceivable that Fenn might have chosen to go in first wicket down instead of opening the batting, but not that he should go in second wicket. If Kay’s were to win it was essential that he should begin to bat as soon as possible. Otherwise there might be no time for him to knock off the runs. However good a batsman is, he can do little if no one can stay with him.

There was no time to question the newcomer. He must control his curiosity until the fall of the next wicket.

“Man in,” he said.

===Chinese===
直到从看台里走出的下一位击球手竟是他弟弟、而不是芬恩时，西尔弗才开始察觉事情不对。芬恩若是不肯开局就上场、而选择第一柱落下后再去，倒还说得通；可第二柱才上场，就说不通了。凯氏要想赢，他必须尽早开始击球，否则可能来不及敲掉那七十九分。击球手再高明，若没人陪他撑着，也是孤掌难鸣。

没工夫盘问新来的这位。他只好把好奇心摁住，等下一柱倒下再说。

「上场。」他说。

===Original===
Billy Silver was in many ways a miniature edition of his brother, and he carried the resemblance into his batting. The head of Blackburn’s was stylish, and took no risks. His brother had not yet developed a style, but he was very settled in his mind on the subject of risks. There was no tempting him with half-volleys and long-hops. His motto was defence, not defiance. He placed a straight bat in the path of every ball, and seemed to consider his duty done if he stopped it.

The remainder of the over was, therefore, quiet. Billy played Kennedy’s fastest like a book, and left the more tempting ones alone.

Challis’s first over realised a single, Wayburn snicking him to leg. The first ball of Kennedy’s second over saw him caught at the wicket, as Walton had been.

“Every *time* a coconut,” said Jimmy Silver complacently, as he walked to the other end. “We’re a powerful combination, Kennedy. Where’s Fenn? Does anybody know? Why doesn’t he come in?”

===Chinese===
比利·西尔弗在许多方面都是他哥哥的袖珍版，这相似还延伸到了击球上。布莱克本的舍长打球姿势漂亮，从不冒险。他弟弟还没练出姿势，可关于冒险这件事，他心里拿定得很。半高球、长跳球休想诱他出棒。他的格言是防守，不是挑衅。他把一根笔直的球棒竖在每一球的来路上，仿佛只要把球挡住，便算尽了本分。

于是这一轮的剩余几球波澜不惊。比利把肯尼迪最快的球打得教科书一般标准，对那些更馋人的球则一概不睬。

查利斯的第一轮得了一分，韦伯恩擦棒到腿侧。肯尼迪第二轮第一球，就见他像沃尔顿一样在门后被告出局。

「真是回回敲得着椰子，」吉米·西尔弗一面朝另一端走去，一面得意洋洋地说，「咱们这个组合威力无穷啊，肯尼迪。芬恩呢？谁知道吗？他怎么还不上场？」

===Original===
Billy Silver, seated on the grass by the side of the crease, fastening the top strap of one of his pads, gave tongue with the eagerness of the well-informed man.

“What, don’t you know?” he said. “Why, there’s been an awful row. Fenn won’t be able to play till four o’clock. I believe he and Kay had a row last night, and he cheeked Kay, and the old man’s given him a sort of extra. I saw him going over to the School House, and I heard him tell Wayburn that he wouldn’t be able to play till four.”

The effect produced by this communication would be most fittingly expressed by the word “sensation” in brackets. It came as a complete surprise to everyone. It seemed to knock the bottom out of the whole match. Without Fenn the thing would be a farce. Kay’s would have no chance.

“What a worm that man is,” said Kennedy. “Do you know, I had a sort of idea Fenn wouldn’t last out much longer. Kay’s been ragging him all the term. I went round to see him last night, and Kay behaved like a bounder then. I expect Fenn had it out with him when they got indoors. What a beastly shame, though.”

===Chinese===
比利·西尔弗正坐在击球区旁的草地上系护腿的上皮带，以消息灵通人士的热切开了腔。

「怎么，你们不知道？」他说，「哎呀，出了大乱子了。芬恩要到四点钟才能上场。我猜他昨晚跟凯先生吵了一架，顶撞了凯先生，老头子就罚了他一场留校补习之类的。我看见他往学校宿舍那边去了，还亲耳听他告诉韦伯恩，说他四点以前上不了场。」

这条消息产生的效果，用加括号的「轰动」二字来形容最为贴切。人人都始料未及。整场比赛仿佛被人抽掉了底。缺了芬恩，这场球就成了闹剧，凯氏毫无胜算。

「那家伙真是个小人，」肯尼迪说，「你知道吗，我早有点预感，芬恩撑不了多久了。凯先生整学期都在找他麻烦。昨晚我去看他，凯先生那做派简直就是个无赖。我估摸着进屋之后，芬恩跟他彻底摊了牌。只是这也太坑人了。」

===Original===
“Beastly,” agreed Jimmy Silver. “Still, it can’t be helped. The sins of the housemaster are visited on the house. I’m afraid it will be our painful duty to wipe the floor with Kay’s this day. Speaking at a venture, I should say that we have got them where the hair’s short. Yea. Even on toast, if I may be allowed to use the expression. Who is this coming forth now? Curtis, or me old eyes deceive me. And is not Curtis’s record score three, marred by ten chances? Indeed yes. A fastish yorker should settle Curtis’s young hash. Try one.”

Kennedy followed the recipe. A ball later the middle and leg stumps were lying in picturesque attitudes some yards behind the crease, and Curtis was beginning that “sad, unending walk to the pavilion,” thinking, with the poet,

“Thou wast not made to play, infernal ball!”

Blackburn’s noncombatants, dotted round the boundary, shrieked their applause. Three wickets had fallen for five runs, and life was worth living. Kay’s were silent and gloomy.

===Chinese===
「是坑人，」吉米·西尔弗附和道，「不过也没办法。舍监造的孽，宿舍来偿还。恐怕把凯氏打得满地找牙，就成了咱们今天一项痛切的义务了。恕我大胆断言，咱们已经掐住了他们毛短的地方。没错，简直可以说把他们搁在烤面包上吃了——如果容我用这个说法的话。这会儿出来的是谁？柯蒂斯（Curtis），要么就是我这双老眼花了。柯蒂斯的最高得分纪录莫非是三分，还附带十次漏接？千真万确。一记略快的约克球（yorker）该够柯蒂斯这小子结账了。来一个。」

肯尼迪照方抓药。一球之后，中柱和腿柱正以多姿的姿态横躺在击球区后方几码处，柯蒂斯则踏上了那段「凄伤而无尽头的走回看台之路」，心里同诗人一道想着：

「尔生原不为击球，可憎之球！」

布莱克本的观战人众散布在边界四周，尖声喝彩。三柱落而五分未出，人生在世，夫复何求。凯氏那边则又沉默又灰暗。

===Original===
Billy Silver continued to occupy one end in an immovable manner, but at the other there was no monotony. Man after man came in, padded and gloved, and looking capable of mighty things. They took guard, patted the ground lustily, as if to make it plain that they were going to stand no nonsense, settled their caps over their eyes, and prepared to receive the ball. When it came it usually took a stump or two with it before it stopped. It was a procession such as the school grounds had not often seen. As the tenth man walked from the pavilion, four sounded from the clock over the Great Hall, and five minutes later the weary eyes of the supporters of Kay’s were refreshed by the sight of Fenn making his way to the arena from the direction of the School House.

===Chinese===
比利·西尔弗继续以雷打不动之势占据着一端，另一端却毫不单调。一人接一人地登场，护腿手套齐备，个个看着都像能干出一番大事业。他们站定击球线，气壮山河地拍拍地面，仿佛要声明自己绝不受人戏弄，把帽檐压到眼睛上，准备接球。而球一到，往往连拐带一兩根门柱才能停住。这样的鱼贯退场大游行，学校球场上还不多见。第十个人从看台走出时，大礼堂的钟敲了四下；五分钟后，凯氏拥护者们疲惫的双眼一亮——芬恩正从学校宿舍那边向战场走来。

===Original===
Just as he arrived on the scene, Billy Silver’s defence broke down. One of Challis’s slows, which he had left alone with the idea that it was going to break away to the off, came in quickly instead, and removed a bail. Billy Silver had only made eight; but, as the full score, including one bye, was only eighteen, this was above the average, and deserved the applause it received.

Fenn came in in the unusual position of eleventh man, with an expression on his face that seemed to suggest that he meant business. He was curiously garbed. Owing to the shortness of the interval allowed him for changing, he had only managed to extend his cricket costume as far as white buckskin boots. He wore no pads or gloves. But even in the face of these sartorial deficiencies, he looked like a cricketer. The field spread out respectfully, and Jimmy Silver moved a man from the slips into the country.

===Chinese===
他刚一到场，比利·西尔弗的防线便失守了。查利斯一记慢球，他本以为会往外偏，索性不接，孰料球急急往里一拐，撞掉了一根横木。比利·西尔弗只得了八分；不过全队总分算上一记「拜伊」也才十八分，这成绩已在平均线之上，赢得的掌声当之无愧。

芬恩以第十一人的罕见身份登场，脸上的神情似乎在说：他此来是动真格的。他的行头颇为别致。留给换装的时间太短，他的板球衣装只来得及置办到白色鹿皮靴这一步。护腿手套一概没有。可即便有这样一身穿着上的缺陷，他看上去仍像个板球手。守场众人心怀敬意地散开，吉米·西尔弗把一个人从滑位区调去了外野腹地。

===Original===
There were three more balls of Challis’s over, for Billy Silver’s collapse had occurred at the third delivery. Fenn mistimed the first. Two hours’ writing indoors does not improve the eye. The ball missed the leg stump by an inch.

About the fifth ball he made no mistake. He got the full face of the bat to it, and it hummed past coverpoint to the boundary. The last of the over he put to leg for three.

A remarkable last-wicket partnership now took place, remarkable not so much for tall scoring as for the fact that one of the partners did not receive a single ball from beginning to end of it, with the exception of the one that bowled him. Fenn seemed to be able to do what he pleased with the bowling. Kennedy he played with a shade more respect than the others, but he never failed to score a three or a single off the last ball of each of his overs. The figures on the telegraph-board rose from twenty to thirty, from thirty to forty, from forty to fifty. Williams went on at the lower end instead of Challis, and Fenn made twelve off his first over. The pavilion was filled with howling enthusiasts, who cheered every hit in a frenzy.

===Chinese===
查利斯那一轮还剩三球，因为比利·西尔弗是在第三投时失守的。第一球芬恩掐错了时机。在屋里抄写两个钟头，于眼力并无裨益。球擦着腿柱偏出一寸。

到第五球他就不失手了。球棒的整个正面结结实实送上，嗡的一声掠过掩切位，直抵边界。这轮最后一球他打向腿侧，跑了三分。

一段异乎寻常的最后一柱搭档赛就此展开。说它非凡，倒不在得分之高，而在于搭档二人中的一位，从头到尾一球未接——除了最后投他出局的那一球。芬恩对付这些投球，简直要怎样就怎样。对肯尼迪他打得比对旁人略多几分敬意，但每轮最后一球，从不失手拿下三分或一分。记分牌上的数字从二十涨到三十，从三十涨到四十，从四十涨到五十。威廉斯接替查利斯在下端投球，芬恩第一轮就从他手里拿下十二分。看台上挤满了狂吼的拥趸，每一记击球都赢得他们疯狂的欢呼。

===Original===
Jimmy Silver began to look worried. He held a hasty consultation with Kennedy. The telegraph-board now showed the figures 60﻿—9﻿—8.

“This won’t do,” said Silver. “It would be too foul to get licked after having nine of them out for eighteen. Can’t you manage to keep Fenn from scoring odd figures off the last ball of your over? If only that kid at the other end would get some of the bowling, we should do it.”

“I’ll try,” said Kennedy, and walked back to begin his over.

===Chinese===
吉米·西尔弗开始面有忧色。他同肯尼迪匆匆计议了一番。记分牌上此刻的数字是 60—9—8。

「这样下去不行，」西尔弗说，「人家十八分丢了九柱，最后反倒输给他们，就太冤了。你就没法子不让芬恩在你每轮最后一球拿单数分？只要那一头的小家伙多少接几球，咱们就赢了。」

「我试试。」肯尼迪说罢走回去开始投球。

===Original===
Fenn reached his fifty off the third ball. Seventy went up on the board. Ten more and Kay’s would have the cup. The fourth ball was too good to hit. Fenn let it pass. The fifth he drove to the on. It was a big hit, but there was a fieldsman in the neighbourhood. Still, it was an easy two. But to Kennedy’s surprise Fenn sent his partner back after they had run a single. Even the umpire was surprised. Fenn’s policy was so obvious that it was strange to see him thus deliberately allow his partner to take a ball.

“That’s not over, you know, Fenn,” said the umpire﻿—Lang, of the School House, a member of the first eleven.

Fenn looked annoyed. He had miscounted the balls, and now his partner, who had no pretensions to be considered a bat, would have to face Kennedy.

That mistake lost Kay’s the match.

===Chinese===
第三球上芬恩拿满了五十分。记分牌升到七十。再得十分，凯氏便可捧杯。第四球太刁，打不得，芬恩放它过去。第五球他打向击球侧。这一击够大，可惜那片地界正守着人。不过跑两分是稳的。谁知令肯尼迪错愕的是，两人跑完一分后，芬恩竟把搭档遣了回去。连裁判都吃了一惊。芬恩的算盘打得一望即知，如今竟眼睁睁看他存心让搭档接球，实在蹊跷。

「这轮还没完呢，芬恩。」裁判说道——他是学校宿舍的朗（Lang），校队成员。

芬恩面有愠色。他数错了球数，而他这位搭档全无击球手可言，这就要直面肯尼迪了。

那一失误，断送了凯氏的比赛。

===Original===
Impossible as he had found it to defeat Fenn, Kennedy had never lost his head or his length. He was bowling fully as well as he had done at the beginning of the innings.

The last ball of the over beat the batsman all the way. He scooped blindly forward, missed it by a foot, and the next moment the off stump lay flat. Blackburn’s had won by seven runs.

===Chinese===
尽管始终奈何不得芬恩，肯尼迪却从未乱了方寸，也未失了准头。他此刻投得同这一局开始时一样出色。

本轮最后一球把击球手彻底骗过。他盲目地向前一兜，差了一尺没碰着球，下一瞬，击球柱应声放平。布莱克本宿舍以七分优势获胜。

## IV Harmony and Discord / 第四章 和谐与失和

===Original===
What might be described as a mixed reception awaited the players as they left the field. The pavilion and the parts about the pavilion rails were always packed on the last day of a final house-match, and even in normal circumstances there was apt to be a little sparring between the juniors of the two houses which had been playing for the cup. In the present case, therefore, it was not surprising that Kay’s fags took the defeat badly. The thought that Fenn’s presence at the beginning of the innings, instead of at the end, would have made all the difference between a loss and a victory, maddened them. The crowd that seethed in front of the pavilion was a turbulent one.

For a time the operation of chairing Fenn up the steps occupied the active minds of the Kayites. When he had disappeared into the first eleven room, they turned their attention in other directions. Caustic and uncomplimentary remarks began to fly to and fro between the representatives of Kay’s and Blackburn’s. It is not known who actually administered the first blow. But, when Fenn came out of the pavilion with Kennedy and Silver, he found a stirring battle in progress. The members of the other houses who had come to look on at the match stood in knots, and gazed with approval at the efforts of Kay’s and Blackburn’s juniors to wipe each other off the face of the earth. The air was full of shrill battle-cries, varied now and then by a smack or a thud, as some young but strenuous fist found a billet. The fortune of war seemed to be distributed equally so far, and the combatants were just warming to their work.

===Chinese===
球员们退场时，等着他们的是一场可谓毁誉参半的接待。宿舍杯决赛的最后一天，看台和看台围栏四周总是挤得水泄不通；即便在平常年景，两只争杯宿舍的低年级生之间也难免要过两招。何况这一回，凯氏的跑腿生们输球输得憋气，也就不足为奇了。一想到芬恩若是在一局开始而非结尾时到场，胜负便会整个倒转，他们简直要疯了。看台前翻腾的人群，是一锅沸腾的粥。

有一阵子，把芬恩抬在椅子上游行上台阶这项工程，占据了凯氏诸君活跃的头脑。等他一消失在一队室里，他们便把注意力转向了别处。刻薄话和不客气的话开始在凯氏与布莱克本两宿舍的代表之间飞来飞去。第一记老拳究竟出自谁手，已不可考。总之，等芬恩同肯尼迪和西尔弗走出看台时，一场热火朝天的战役已然打响。其他宿舍来看比赛的成员三五成群地站着，赞许地观赏凯氏和布莱克本的低年级生互相把对方从地球表面上抹去的努力。空气里满是尖利的战吼，间或点缀一声脆响或闷响——那是某只年少而劲道十足的拳头寻得了雇主。战局到目前为止大体均衡，参战双方才刚刚打出兴致。

===Original===
“Look here,” said Kennedy, “we ought to stop this.”

“What’s the good,” said Fenn, without interest. “It pleases them, and doesn’t hurt anybody else.”

“All the same,” observed Jimmy Silver, moving towards the nearest group of combatants, “free fights aren’t quite the thing, somehow. For, children, you should never let your angry passions rise; your little hands were never made to tear each other’s eyes. Dr. Watts’ *Advice to Young Pugilists*. Drop it, you little beasts.”

He separated two heated youths who were just beginning a fourth round. The rest of the warriors, seeing Silver and the others, called a truce, and Silver, having read a sort of Riot Act, moved on. The juniors of the beaten house, deciding that it would be better not to resume hostilities, consoled themselves by giving three groans for Mr. Kay.

===Chinese===
「我说，」肯尼迪道，「咱们该去制止。」

「有什么用，」芬恩兴味索然地说，「他们高兴，又不碍着别人。」

「话虽如此，」吉米·西尔弗一边朝最近一撮战斗人员走去，一边发表意见，「混战总归有失体统。孩儿们，切勿任怒火中烧；你们的小手，生来不是为了抠瞎彼此的眼睛——沃茨博士（Dr. Watts）《致少年拳手的忠告》。住手，你们这群小畜生。」

他拆开了两个正要开打第四回合的热血少年。其余的战士一见西尔弗等人驾到，便鸣金休兵；西尔弗宣读了一通类似《骚乱法》的训谕，随即继续赶路。败方宿舍的低年级生们掂量再三，决定不宜重启战端，便以朝凯先生发出三声倒彩聊以自慰。

===Original===
“What happened after I left you last night, Fenn?” asked Kennedy.

“Oh, I had one of my usual rows with Kay, only rather worse than usual. I said one or two things he didn’t like, and today the old man sent for me and told me to come to his room from two till four. Kay had run me in for being ‘grossly rude.’ Listen to those kids. What a row they’re making!”

“It’s a beastly shame,” said Kennedy despondently.

At the school shop Morrell, of Mulholland’s, met them. He had been spending the afternoon with a rug and a novel on the hills at the back of the school, and he wanted to know how the final house-match had gone. Blackburn’s had beaten Mulholland’s in one of the early rounds. Kennedy explained what had happened.

===Chinese===
「昨晚我走了以后又怎么了，芬恩？」肯尼迪问。

「哦，我跟凯先生照例吵了一架，只是比往常凶了些。我说了一两句他不爱听的话，今天老头子就派人叫我去，罚我两点到四点在他屋里待着。凯先生给我记了一过，罪名是『大不敬』。听听那帮小的，吵得多欢！」

「真是太坑人了。」肯尼迪垂头丧气地说。

在学校小卖部门口，马尔霍兰宿舍的莫雷尔（Morrell）迎上了他们。他一下午都披着毯子、拿着一本小说，在学校后头的山丘上消磨，正想打听宿舍杯决赛的结果如何。布莱克本宿舍在早先某一轮里赢过马尔霍兰宿舍。肯尼迪把经过讲了一遍。

===Original===
“We should have lost if Fenn had turned up earlier,” he said. “He had a row with Kay, and Kay gave him a sort of extra between two and four.”

Fenn, busily occupied with an ice, added no comment of his own to this plain tale.

“Rough luck,” said Morrell. “What’s all that row out in the field?”

“That’s Kay’s kids giving three groans for Kay,” explained Silver. “At least, they started with the idea of giving three groans. They’ve got up to about three hundred by this time. It seems to have fascinated them. They won’t leave off. There’s no school rule against groaning in the grounds, and they mean to groan till the end of the term. Personally, I like the sound. But then, I’m fond of music.”

===Chinese===
「芬恩要是早点到场，我们就输了，」他说，「他跟凯先生吵了一架，凯先生罚他两点到四点留校补习了一场。」

芬恩正忙着对付一份冰淇淋，对这段朴实的叙述没有补充任何个人意见。

「够倒霉的，」莫雷尔说，「场子上那阵吵嚷是怎么回事？」

「那是凯氏的娃娃们在向凯先生致三声倒彩，」西尔弗解释道，「至少，他们起初的构想是三声。到现在已经喊到三百声上下了。这活动似乎把他们迷住了，怎么也不肯停。校规里没有一条禁止在校园里发嘘声，他们打算一路嘘到学期结束。就我个人而言，我爱听这动静。不过嘛，我这人本来就爱好音乐。」

===Original===
Morrell’s face beamed with sudden pleasure. “I knew there was something I wanted to tell you,” he said, “only I couldn’t remember what. Your saying you’re fond of music reminds me. Mulholland’s crocked himself, and won’t be able to turn out for the concert.”

“What!” cried Kennedy. “How did it happen? What’s he done?”

Mr. Mulholland was the master who looked after the music of the school, a fine cricketer and keen sportsman. Had nothing gone wrong, he would have conducted at the concert that night.

“I heard it from the matron at our place,” said Morrell. “She’s full of it. Mulholland was batting at the middle net, and somebody else﻿—I forget who﻿—was at the one next to it on the right. The bowler sent down a long-hop to leg, and this Johnny had a smack at it, and sent it slap through the net, and it got Mulholland on the side of the head. He was stunned for a bit, but he’s getting all right again now. But he won’t be able to conduct tonight. Rather bad luck on the man, especially as he’s so keen on the concert.”

===Chinese===
莫雷尔的脸上忽然绽开喜色。「我就知道有件事要告诉你们，」他说，「只是一时想不起来是什么。你一说爱好音乐，我就想起来了。马尔霍兰先生把自己弄伤了，今晚的音乐会来不了啦。」

「什么！」肯尼迪叫道，「怎么弄的？他怎么了？」

马尔霍兰先生（Mr. Mulholland）是主管全校音乐的教员，板球好手，热衷运动。若非出了这桩意外，当晚的音乐会本该由他执棒。

「我是听我们宿舍的女管家说的，」莫雷尔道，「她讲得可起劲了。马尔霍兰先生正在中间的网练场练击球，另有一个人——忘了是谁——在右边隔壁那个网。投手投来一记腿侧长跳球，这家伙抡了一记，把球结结实实打穿了球网，正中马尔霍兰先生的脑袋侧面。他昏了一会儿，现在渐渐好了。可他今晚执不了棒了。这人也是够倒霉的，何况他对这场音乐会上心得不得了。」

===Original===
“Who’s going to sub for him?” asked Silver. “Perhaps they’ll scratch the show,” suggested Kennedy.

“Oh, no,” said Morrell, “it’s all right. Kay is going to conduct. He’s often done it at choir practices when Mulholland couldn’t turn up.”

Fenn put down his empty saucer with an emphatic crack on the counter.

“If Kay’s going to run the show, I’m hanged if I turn up,” he said.

“My dear chap, you can’t get out of it now,” said Kennedy anxiously. He did not want to see Fenn plunging into any more strife with the authorities this term.

===Chinese===
「谁去顶他的班？」西尔弗问。「没准他们会把演出取消。」肯尼迪提议道。

「哦，不会，」莫雷尔说，「没问题的。凯先生要来执棒。马尔霍兰先生来不了的时候，唱诗班排练常是他代劳。」

芬恩把空碟子往柜台上一放，啪的一声，掷地有声。

「要是凯先生来主持这台戏，打死我也不去。」他说。

「老兄，事到如今你可临阵脱不得，」肯尼迪焦急地说。他不愿看到芬恩在这个学期再同当局起任何冲突。

===Original===
“Think of the crowned heads who are coming to hear you,” pleaded Jimmy Silver. “Think of the nobility and gentry. Think of me. You must play.”

“Ah, there you are, Fenn.”

Mr. Kay had bustled in in his energetic way.

Fenn said nothing. He *was* there. It was idle to deny it.

“I thought I should find you here. Yes, I wanted to see you about the concert tonight. Mr. Mulholland has met with an unfortunate accident, and I am looking after the entertainment in his place. Come with me and play over your piece. I should like to see that you are perfect in it. Dear me, dear me, what a noise those boys are making. Why *are* they behaving in that extraordinary way, I wonder!”

===Chinese===
「想想那些要来听你演奏的戴王冠的头颅吧，」吉米·西尔弗恳求道，「想想贵族与士绅。想想我。你必须上场。」

「啊，原来你在这儿，芬恩。」

凯先生风风火火地闯了进来。

芬恩一言不发。他确实在这儿，抵赖也是枉然。

「我就料到能在这儿找到你。是这样，今晚的音乐会，我有话同你说。马尔霍兰先生不幸出了事故，娱乐节目由我代为照管。跟我来，把你的曲子过一遍。我要亲眼看着你把它弄熟。哎呀，哎呀，那些孩子闹得多厉害。他们到底为什么这样反常，我真纳闷！」

===Original===
Kay’s juniors had left the pavilion, and were trooping back to their house. At the present moment they were passing the school shop, and their tuneful voices floated in through the open window.

“This is very unusual. Why, they seem to be boys in my house. They are groaning.”

“I think they are a little upset at the result of the match, sir,” said Jimmy Silver suavely. “Fenn did not arrive, for some reason, till the end of the innings, so Mr. Blackburn’s won. The wicket was good, but a little fiery.”

“Thank you, Silver,” replied Mr. Kay with asperity. “When I require explanations I will ask for them.”

He darted out of the shop, and a moment later they heard him pouring out a flood of recriminations on the groaning fags.

===Chinese===
凯氏的低年级生已经离开看台，正成群结队往宿舍走。这会儿恰好经过学校小卖部，他们悠扬的歌声从敞开的窗口飘了进来。

「这可太反常了。咦，这些好像是我宿舍的学生。他们在发嘘声。」

「我想他们是对比赛结果有点儿不痛快，先生，」吉米·西尔弗温文尔雅地说，「芬恩不知怎么搞的，直到那局快结束才到场，所以布莱克本先生那边赢了。球场很好，就是有点火爆。」

「多谢你的解说，西尔弗，」凯先生厉声答道，「需要人解说的时候，我自会开口。」

他一阵风似的冲出小卖部；片刻之后，众人便听见他朝发嘘声的跑腿生们倾泻出一连串的申斥。

===Original===
“There was *once* a man who snubbed me,” said Jimmy Silver. “They buried him at Brookwood. Well, what are you going to do, Fenn? Going to play tonight? Harkee, boy. Say but the word, and I will beard this tyrant to his face.”

Fenn rose.

“Yes,” he said briefly, “I shall play. You’d better turn up. I think you’ll enjoy it.”

Silver said that no human power should keep him away.

===Chinese===
「从前*有*一个人扫过我的兴，」吉米·西尔弗说，「他们把他埋在布鲁克伍德（Brookwood）了。好了，你打算怎么办，芬恩？今晚还弹不弹？听着，小子。只要你说个字，我就当着这暴君的面捋他的虎须。」

芬恩站起身来。

「弹，」他简短地说，「我会上场的。你们最好也都来。我想你们会不虚此行。」

西尔弗表示，人间已没有任何力量能把他拦在家里。

---

===Original===
The School concert was always one of the events of the summer term. There was a concert at the end of the winter term, too, but it was not so important. To a great many of those present the summer concert marked, as it were, the last flutter of their school life. On the morrow they would be Old Boys, and it behoved them to extract as much enjoyment from the function as they could. Under Mr. Mullholland’s rule the concert had become a very flourishing institution. He aimed at a high standard, and reached it. There was more than a touch of the austere about the music. A glance at the programme was enough to show the lover of airs of the trashy, clashy order that this was no place for him. Most of the items were serious. When it was thought necessary to introduce a lighter touch, some staidly rollicking number was inserted, some song that was saved﻿—in spite of a catchy tune﻿—by a halo of antiquity. Anything modern was taboo, unless it were the work of Gotsuchakoff, Thingummyowsky, or some other eminent foreigner. Foreign origin made it just possible.

The school prefects lurked during the performance at the doors and at the foot of the broad stone steps that led to the Great Hall. It was their duty to supply visitors with programmes.

===Chinese===
学校音乐会向来是夏季学期的一件大事。冬季学期末也有一场音乐会，但没这么要紧。对在场许多人来说，夏季音乐会仿佛标志着他们学生生涯的最后一场雀跃。明天他们就是老校友了，理应从这项活动中榨取尽可能多的乐趣。在马尔霍兰先生的治理下，音乐会已成了一项蔚为大观的制度。他志在高标准，而且达到了。这里的音乐不无几分肃穆之气。爱好者听之欲醉的那种喧闹俚俗的曲调，只要对节目单看上一眼，便知此地非其乐土。节目大多庄重。偶有调剂轻快之需，也是插一支稳重的诙谐曲，某首靠着古老年代的光环得救的歌——纵然曲调抓人。一切现代货色都在禁忌之列，除非出自戈楚卡科夫（Gotsuchakoff）、廷加莫夫斯基（Thingummyowsky）或其他某位显赫外国人的手笔。外国出身，勉强算是通了融。

演出期间，学校的级长们埋伏在各道门口，以及通往大礼堂的宽阔石阶脚下。他们的职责是给来宾发放节目单。

===Original===
Jimmy Silver had foregathered with Kennedy, Challis, and Williams at the junior door. The hall was full now, and their labours consequently at an end.

“Pretty good ‘gate,’ ” said Silver, looking in through the open door. “It must be warm up in the gallery.”

Across the further end of the hall a dais had been erected. On this the bulk of the school sat, leaving the body of the hall to the crowned heads, nobility, and gentry to whom Silver had referred in his conversation with Fenn.

“It always is warm in the gallery,” said Challis. “I lost about two stone there every concert when I was a kid. We simply used to sit and melt.”

===Chinese===
吉米·西尔弗同肯尼迪、查利斯和威廉斯聚在低年级门口。礼堂这时已经坐满，他们的差事也就告终。

「上座率相当不错，」西尔弗从敞开的门往里张望着说，「楼座里一定热坏了。」

礼堂另一端搭起了一座高台，全校学生的大部队坐在台上，把礼堂正身留给西尔弗在同芬恩谈话时提过的那些戴王冠的头颅、贵族与士绅。

「楼座里向来热，」查利斯说，「我小时候每开一场音乐会，都要在那上头瘦掉差不多两英石。我们简直就是坐着化掉。」

===Original===
“And I tell you what,” broke in Silver, “it’s going to get warmer before the end of the show. Do you notice that all Kay’s house are sitting in a lump at the back. I bet they’re simply spoiling for a row. Especially now Kay’s running the concert. There’s going to be a hot time in the old town tonight﻿—you see if there isn’t. Hark at ’em.”

The choir had just come to the end of a little thing of Handel’s. There was no reason to suppose that the gallery appreciated Handel. Nevertheless, they were making a deafening noise. Clouds of dust rose from the rhythmical stamping of many feet. The noise was loudest and the dust thickest by the big window, beneath which sat the men from Kay’s. Things were warming up.

The gallery, with one last stamp which nearly caused the dais to collapse, quieted down. The masters in the audience looked serious. One or two of the visitors glanced over their shoulders with a smile. How excited the dear boys were at the prospect of holidays! Young blood! Young blood! Boys *would* be boys.

The concert continued. Halfway through the programme there was a ten minutes’ interval. Fenn’s pianoforte solo was the second item of the second half.

===Chinese===
「还有我跟你说，」西尔弗插话道，「散场之前，那上头只会更热。你们注意到没有，凯氏全班人马都抱团坐在后排。我敢打赌，他们简直就憋着要闹一场。尤其是如今音乐会归了凯先生主持。今晚这老城有热闹瞧了——不信走着瞧。听听他们。」

唱诗班刚唱完亨德尔（Handel）的一支小曲。没有理由认为楼座懂得欣赏亨德尔。可他们照旧闹出震耳欲聋的动静。尘土随着许多双脚的节奏踏动腾腾而起。噪声最响、尘土最浓的地方是大窗户底下，凯氏诸君正坐在那里。事态正在升温。

楼座又猛跺了最后一脚——差点没把高台跺塌——总算安静下来。观众席上的教员们面色凝重。一两位来宾回头笑了笑，暗道：亲爱的小伙子们一想到放假就这么兴奋！年轻气盛！年轻气盛！男孩子嘛，*天性*如此。

音乐会继续进行。节目过半，有十分钟的中场休息。芬恩的钢琴独奏排在下半场第二支。

===Original===
He mounted the platform amidst howls of delight from the gallery. Applause at the Eckleton concerts was granted more for services in the playing-fields than merit as a musician. Kubelik or Paderewski would have been welcomed with a few polite handclaps. A man in the eleven or fifteen was certain of two minutes’ unceasing cheers.

“Evidently one of their heroes, my dear,” said Paterfamilias to Materfamilias. “I suppose he has won a scholarship at the University.”

Paterfamilias’ mind was accustomed to run somewhat upon scholarships at the University. What the school wanted was a batting average of forty odd or a bowling analysis in single figures.

Fenn played the “Moonlight Sonata.” A trained musical critic would probably have found much to cavil at in his rendering of the piece, but it was undoubtedly good for a public school player. Of course he was encored. The gallery would have encored him if he had played with one finger, three mistakes to every bar.

===Chinese===
他在楼座的一片欢呼声中登上台去。在埃克尔顿的音乐会上，掌声更多是发给运动场上的功勋，而非音乐上的造诣。库贝利克（Kubelik）或帕岱莱夫斯基（Paderewski）来了，也只会博得几声礼貌的轻拍；而一个校队板球队或橄榄球队的人，则稳拿两分钟不绝的欢呼。

「这分明是他们的一位英雄，亲爱的，」一位一家之主对太太说，「我猜他准是得了大学的奖学金。」

这位一家之主的脑子，习惯了围着大学奖学金打转。而学校真正要的，是四十上下的击球平均分，或个位数的投球数据。

芬恩弹的是《月光奏鸣曲》。训练有素的音乐评论家在他的演绎里多半能挑出一大堆毛病，但以公学水准而论，无疑相当出色。返场自然免不了。哪怕他用一根手指弹，每小节错三个音，楼座也照样会叫他返场。

===Original===
“I told Fenn,” said Jimmy Silver, “if he got an encore, that he ought to play the﻿—My aunt! *He is!*”

Three runs and half-a-dozen crashes, and there was no further room for doubt. Fenn was playing “The Coon Band Contest.”

“He’s gone mad,” gasped Kennedy.

Whether he had or not, it is certain that the gallery had. All the evening they had been stewing in an atmosphere like that of the inner room of a Turkish bath, and they were ready for anything. It needed but a trifle to set them off. The lilt of that unspeakable Yankee melody supplied that trifle. Kay’s malcontents, huddled in their seats by the window, were the first to break out. Feet began to stamp in time to the music﻿—softly at first, then more loudly. The wooden dais gave out the sound like a drum.

===Chinese===
「我跟芬恩说过，」吉米·西尔弗道，「要是返场，他就该弹那支——我的天！*他真弹了！*」

三串音阶跑动，六记重砸，再无疑问的余地。芬恩弹的正是《黑人乐队大赛》。

「他疯了。」肯尼迪倒抽一口气。

他疯没疯且不论，楼座是肯定疯了。他们一整晚都焖在土耳其浴室里间一般的空气中，早已万事俱备，只欠东风。一点火星就能点着。那支无法言说的扬基小曲的轻快节拍，恰好供上了这点火星。挤在窗边座位上的凯氏愤懑分子率先发难。双脚开始合着乐声踏拍——起先还轻，继而大作。木高台发出的声响，活像一面大鼓。

===Original===
Other rioters joined in from the right. The noise spread through the gallery as a fire spreads through gorse. Soon three hundred pairs of well-shod feet were rising and falling. Somebody began to whistle. Everybody whistled. Mr. Kay was on his feet, gesticulating wildly. His words were lost in the uproar.

For five minutes the din prevailed. Then, with a final crash, Fenn finished. He got up from the music-stool, bowed, and walked back to his place by the senior door. The musical efforts of the gallery changed to a storm of cheering and clapping.

The choir rose to begin the next piece.

Still the noise continued.

People began to leave the Hall﻿—in ones and twos first, then in a steady stream which blocked the doorways. It was plain to the dullest intelligence that if there was going to be any more concert, it would have to be performed in dumb show. Mr. Kay flung down his baton.

===Chinese===
右翼的暴动分子也加入进来。噪声在楼座里蔓延，如同野火燎过荆豆丛。不多时，三百双皮鞋裹着的脚一齐起落。有人吹起了口哨。人人都吹起了口哨。凯先生霍然起身，狂乱地打着手势。他的话音淹没在喧嚣里。

喧嚣肆虐了五分钟。随后，随着最后一声重砸，芬恩收了曲。他从琴凳上起身，鞠了一躬，走回高年级门旁自己的座位。楼座的音乐事业，转成了一场欢呼加鼓掌的风暴。

唱诗班起身，准备开始下一支曲子。

喧嚣依然如故。

人们开始退场——起先三三两两，继而川流不息，把各道门口堵了个严实。最迟钝的头脑也看得分明：这音乐会若还要开下去，只能改演哑剧了。凯先生把指挥棒狠狠一摔。

===Original===
The visitors had left by now, and the gallery was beginning to follow their example, howling as it went.

“Well,” said Jimmy Silver cheerfully, as he went with Kennedy down the steps, “I *think* we may call that a record. By my halidom, there’ll be a row about this later on.”

===Chinese===
来宾此时已散尽，楼座也开始效法他们，一路呼啸而去。

「哎，」吉米·西尔弗同肯尼迪走下台阶时，愉快地说，「我*想*咱们可以管这叫一项纪录了。凭我的圣物起誓，这事往后可有得闹。」

## V Camp / 第五章 军营

===Original===
With the best intentions in the world, however, a headmaster cannot make a row about a thing unless he is given a reasonable amount of time to make it in. The concert being on the last evening of term, there was only a single morning before the summer holidays, and that morning was occupied with the prize-giving. The school assembled at ten o’clock with a shadowy hope that this prize-day would be more exciting than the general run of prize-days, but they were disappointed. The function passed off without sensation. The headmaster did not denounce the school in an impassioned speech from the dais. He did not refer to the events of the previous evening. At the same time, his demeanour was far from jovial. It lacked that rollicking bonhomie which we like to see in headmasters on prize-day. It was evident to the most casual observer that the affair was not closed. The school would have to pay the bill sooner or later. But eight weeks would elapse before the day of reckoning, which was a comforting thought.

The last prize was handed over to its rightful owner. The last and dullest vote of thanks had been proposed by the last and dullest member of the board of governors. The Bishop of Rumtifoo (who had been selected this year to distribute the prizes) had worked off his seventy minutes’ speech (inaudible, of course, as usual), and was feeling much easier. The term had been formally declared at an end, and those members of the school corps who were going to camp were beginning to assemble in front of the buildings.

===Chinese===
然而，一位校长纵有世上最良善的用意，若没有一段宽裕的时间可供发作，也无从为一件事大闹一场。音乐会排在学期最后一晚，暑假之前便只剩一个上午，而这个上午又被发奖典礼占去了。全校十点钟集合，心里揣着一丝朦胧的希望：这回的颁奖日总该比寻常的颁奖日精彩些吧。结果他们失望了。典礼波澜不惊地过去了。校长没有在高台上发表慷慨激昂的演说痛斥全校，也绝口不提前一晚的事件。可与此同时，他的神气又绝算不上愉快。颁奖日的校长身上我们爱看的那种乐呵呵的豪爽劲儿，一概欠奉。再漫不经心的旁观者也看得出：此事未了。全校迟早要付这笔账。不过结账日之前还隔着八个礼拜，倒是一桩宽心的事。

最后一份奖品交到了它名正言顺的主人手里。最末也最乏味的那通谢词，已由校董会里最末也最乏味的一位成员提了出来。拉姆蒂福主教（Bishop of Rumtifoo）（今年选定的发奖人）把他七十分钟的演讲（照例当然没人听得见）讲完下台，通体舒泰了不少。学期正式宣告结束，学生军里要去军营的那些人，开始在楼前集合。

===Original===
“I wonder why it always takes about three hours to get us off to the station,” said Jimmy Silver. “I’ve been to camp two years now, and there’s always been this rotting about in the grounds before we start. Nobody’s likely to turn up to inspect us for the next hour or so. If any gent cares to put in a modest ginger-beer at the shop, I’m with him.”

“I don’t see why we shouldn’t,” said Kennedy. He had seen Fenn go into the shop, and wished to talk to him. He had not seen him after the concert, and he thought it would be interesting to know how Kay had taken it, and what his comments had been on meeting Fenn in the house that night.

Fenn had not much to say.

“He was rather worried,” he said, grinning as if the recollection of the interview amused him. “But he couldn’t do anything. Of course, there’ll be a row next term, but it can’t be helped.”

===Chinese===
「我就纳闷，送咱们上火车站怎么总要花上三个钟头，」吉米·西尔弗说，「我进军营都两年了，每回出发前都得在校园里这么干耗。接下来个把钟头，反正不会有人来检阅咱们。哪位先生若肯赏光去小卖部来一瓶朴素点的姜汁汽水，我奉陪。」

「我看没什么不行的。」肯尼迪说。他看见芬恩进了小卖部，正想找他聊聊。音乐会后他还没见过他；他想，凯先生如何消化那件事、当晚在宿舍碰见芬恩时发表了怎样的高论，打听一下想必有趣。

芬恩没什么可说的。

「他挺犯愁的，」他说，咧嘴笑着，仿佛回忆起那场交锋就觉得有趣，「可他无计可施。下学期自然有一场风波，那也没办法。」

===Original===
“If I were you,” said Silver, “I should point out to them that you’d a perfect right to play what you liked for an encore. How were you to know the gallery would go off like that? You aren’t responsible for them. Hullo, there’s that bugle. Things seem to be on the move. We must go.”

“So long,” said Fenn.

“Goodbye. Mind you come off against Middlesex.”

===Chinese===
「我要是你，」西尔弗说，「就跟他们指出：返场弹什么，你享有全权。你哪知道楼座会那样发作？他们又不归你负责。喂，号响了。看样子要动身了。咱们得走了。」

「回见。」芬恩说。

「再见。对米德尔塞克斯那场可要好好打。」

===Original===
Kennedy stayed for a moment.

“Has the Old Man said anything to you yet?” he asked.

“Not yet. He’ll do that next term. It’ll be something to look forward to.”

Kennedy hurried off to take his place in the ranks.

Getting to camp at the end of the summer term is always a nuisance. Aldershot seems a long way from everywhere, and the trains take their time over the journey. Then, again, the heat always happens to be particularly oppressive on that day. Snow may have fallen on the day before, but directly one sets out for camp, the thermometer goes up into three figures. The Eckleton contingent marched into the lines damp and very thirsty.

===Chinese===
肯尼迪又留了片刻。

「老头子找你说过话没有？」他问。

「还没有。下学期才轮到。有盼头了。」

肯尼迪匆匆跑去归队。

夏季学期结束时赶赴军营，向来是件苦差。奥尔德肖特（Aldershot）这地方仿佛离哪儿都远，火车又不紧不慢地磨蹭。再者，那天天气总归格外闷热。头一天没准还下着雪，可人一动身去军营，温度计立刻蹿上三位数。埃克尔顿分队开进营地时，人人汗津津的，渴得要命。

===Original===
Most of the other schools were already on the spot, and looked as if they had been spending the last few years there. There was nothing particular going on when the Eckleton warriors arrived, and everybody was lounging about in khaki and shirtsleeves, looking exasperatingly cool. The only consolation which buoyed up the spirits of Eckleton was the reflection that in a short space of time, when the important-looking gentleman in uniform who had come to meet them had said all he wanted to say on the subject of rules and regulations, they would be like that too. Happy thought! If the man bucked up and cut short the peroration, there would be time for a bathe in Cove Reservoir. Those of the corps who had been to camp in previous years felt quite limp with the joy of the thought. Why couldn’t he get through with it, and give a fellow a chance of getting cool again?

The gist of the oration was apparently that the Eckleton cadets were to consider themselves not only as soldiers﻿—and as such subject to military discipline, and the rules for the conduct of troops quartered in the Aldershot district﻿—but also as members of a public school. In short, that if they misbehaved themselves they would get cells, and a hundred lines in the same breath, as it were.

===Chinese===
其余学校大多已经到场，一副在当地住了好几年的模样。埃克尔顿的勇士们到达时，营里没什么特别的动静，人人穿着卡其军装、挽着袖子闲逛，一脸凉爽得叫人来气。支撑埃克尔顿士气的唯一安慰是这样一个念头：用不了多久，等那位前来迎接、相貌威严的军装绅士把规章制度方面想讲的话统统讲完，他们也会是那副模样。好念头！这人若是识相，把结束语砍短些，还来得及去科夫水库（Cove Reservoir）泡一泡。军中往年进过营的那几位，想到这里简直快乐得浑身发软。他怎么就不能快点讲完，放人一个凉快凉快的机会呢？

那篇训示的要旨，大意是说埃克尔顿的学员们应当把自己不但看作士兵——因此须受军纪以及驻奥尔德肖特地区部队行为准则的约束——而且看作公学的一员。简而言之，一旦行为不端，便会一边关禁闭，一边罚抄一百行，两不耽误。

===Original===
The corps knew all this ages ago. The man seemed to think he was telling them something fresh. They began positively to dislike him after a while.

He finished at last. Eckleton marched off wearily, but in style, to its lines.

“Dis‑miss!”

They did.

“And about time, too,” said Jimmy Silver. “I wish they would tie that man up, or something. He’s one of the worst bores I know. He may be full of bright conversation in private life, but in public he will talk about his beastly military regulations. You can’t stop him. It’s a perfect mania with him. Now, I believe﻿—that is to say, I have a sort of dim idea﻿—that there’s a place round about here called a canteen. I seem to remember such a thing vaguely. We might go and look for it.”

===Chinese===
这些道理，全军早就烂熟于心。此人却似乎自认为在向他们传授新鲜学问。过了一阵子，大家当真开始讨厌起他来。

他总算讲完了。埃克尔顿疲惫然而有型地开回自己的营地。

「解——散！」

他们解散了。

「也该到时候了，」吉米·西尔弗说，「真希望他们把那人捆起来，或者想点什么办法。他是我认识的最能闷人的家伙之一。私底下他也许妙语连珠，可一到人前，他就非讲他那套要命的军规不可。拦都拦不住。简直是一种癖好。这么说吧，我相信——也就是说，我隐约有个模糊的印象——这附近有个地方，叫做小卖部。我似乎依稀记得有这么个所在。咱们不妨去找找看。」

===Original===
Kennedy made no objection.

This was his first appearance at camp. Jimmy Silver, on the other hand, was a veteran. He had been there twice before, and meant to go again. He had a peculiar and extensive knowledge of the ins and outs of the place. Kennedy was quite willing to take him as his guide. He was full of information. Kennedy was surprised to see what a number of men from the other schools he seemed to know. In the canteen there were, amongst others, a Carthusian, two Tonbridge men, and a Haileyburian. They all greeted Silver with the warmth of old friends.

“You get to know a lot of fellows in camp,” explained Jimmy, as they strolled back to the Eckleton lines. “That’s the best of the place. Camp’s the best place on earth, if only you have decent weather. See that chap over there? He came here last year. He’d never been before, and one of the things he didn’t know was that Cove Reservoir’s only about three feet deep round the sides. He took a running dive, and almost buried himself in the mud. It’s about two feet deep. He told me afterwards he swallowed pounds of it. Rather bad luck. Somebody ought to have told him. You can’t do much diving here.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪无异议。

这是他头一回进军营。吉米·西尔弗则是一位老兵油子，之前来过两回，往后还打算再来。他对这地方的沟沟坎坎，有着一份独到而渊博的学问。肯尼迪乐得请他做向导。他满腹都是情报。肯尼迪惊奇地发现，别校的人他竟识得那么多。小卖部里当时坐着一位查特豪斯人（Carthusian）、两位汤布里奇人（Tonbridge），还有一位海利伯里人（Haileyburian）。他们个个都以老友的热忱向西尔弗打招呼。

「进军营就能结识一大帮人，」吉米一面同他踱回埃克尔顿营地，一面解释说，「这是此地的一大妙处。只要天气像样，军营是天下第一等的好地方。看见那边那家伙了吗？他去年来的。以前从没来过，他不知道的事情里有一件是：科夫水库边上只有三英尺深。他一个助跑扎了下去，差点把自己埋进泥里。那儿只有两英尺深。他后来跟我说，他咽下去的泥有好几磅。够倒霉的。总该有人事先告诉他一声。在这儿跳水是跳不出什么名堂的。」

===Original===
“Glad you mentioned it,” said Kennedy. “I should have dived myself if you hadn’t.”

Many other curious and diverting facts did the expert drag from the bonded warehouse of his knowledge. Nothing changes at camp. Once get to know the ropes, and you know them for all time.

“The one thing I bar,” he said, “is having to get up at half-past five. And one day in the week, when there’s a divisional field-day, it’s half-past four. It’s hardly worth while going to sleep at all. Still, it isn’t so bad as it used to be. The first year I came to camp we used to have to do a three hours’ field-day before brekker. We used to have coffee before it, and nothing else till it was over. By Jove, you felt you’d had enough of it before you got back. This is Laffan’s Plain. The worst of Laffan’s Plain is that you get to know it too well. You get jolly sick of always starting on field-days from the same place, and marching across the same bit of ground. Still, I suppose they can’t alter the scenery for our benefit. See that man there? He won the sabres at Aldershot last year. That chap with him is in the Clifton footer team.”

===Chinese===
「幸亏你提了这一嘴，」肯尼迪说，「不然我自己也要跳的。」

这位行家又从他那座保税仓库般的知识库里，拖出了许多别的奇闻趣事。军营里什么都不带变的。门道一旦摸熟，终身受用。

「我唯一抵制的一项，」他说，「是五点半就得起床。一礼拜还有一天，师级野战日，四点半。简直犯不着睡觉。不过，比起从前已经不算苦了。我头一年来军营，早饭前得先来一场三个钟头的野战日。战前只给一杯咖啡，此外什么都没有，直到演习结束。老天，没等回营你就觉得受够了。这是拉凡平原（Laffan’s Plain）。拉凡平原最糟的是你会把它认得太熟。野战日永远从同一个地方出发，行军永远穿过同一块地，腻都腻死了。不过，想必他们不能专为咱们把布景换一换。看见那边那人了吗？他去年在奥尔德肖特赢了军剑冠军。跟他一起的那位，是克利夫顿橄榄球队的。」

===Original===
When a school corps goes to camp, it lives in a number of tents, and, as a rule, each house collects in a tent of its own. Blackburn’s had a tent, and further down the line Kay’s had assembled. The Kay contingent were under Wayburn, a good sort, as far as he himself was concerned, but too weak to handle a mob like Kay’s. Wayburn was not coming back after the holidays, a fact which perhaps still further weakened his hold on the Kayites. They had nothing to fear from him next term.

Kay’s was represented at camp by a dozen or so of its members, of whom young Billy Silver alone had any pretensions to the esteem of his fellow man. Kay’s was the rowdiest house in the school, and the cream of its rowdy members had come to camp. There was Walton, for one, a perfect specimen of the public school man at his worst. There was Mortimer, another of Kay’s gems. Perry, again, and Callingham, and the rest. A pleasant gang, fit for anything, if it could be done in safety.

===Chinese===
学生军到了军营，住的是一片帐篷，照例每个宿舍自聚一顶。布莱克本宿舍占了一顶，再往营列那头，凯氏宿舍的人也聚齐了。凯氏分队的头儿是韦伯恩。就他自身而论，人不坏，只是要镇住凯氏这样一群乌合之众，未免太软。韦伯恩假期后就不回来了，这大概进一步削弱了他对凯氏人的约束力。下学期，他们没什么好怕他的了。

凯氏宿舍来军营的，是十来号人，其中唯有小比利·西尔弗还够得上同胞的几分敬重。凯氏是全校最吵闹的宿舍，吵闹分子中的精华此次悉数到场。有沃尔顿，公学学生最恶劣形态的完美标本。有莫蒂默（Mortimer），凯氏又一粒明珠。还有佩里（Perry）、卡林厄姆（Callingham），等等等等。一群可人儿，只要没有风险，什么匀当都干得出来。

===Original===
Kennedy observed them, and﻿—the spectacle starting a train of thought﻿—asked Jimmy Silver, as they went into their tent just before lights-out, if there was much ragging in camp.

“Not very much,” said the expert. “Chaps are generally too done up at the end of the day to want to do anything except sleep. Still, I’ve known cases. You sometimes get one tent mobbing another. They loose the ropes, you know. Low trick, I think. It isn’t often done, and it gets dropped on like bricks when it’s found out. But why? Do you feel as if you wanted to do it?”

“It only occurred to me that we’ve got a lively gang from Kay’s here. I was wondering if they’d get any chances of ragging, or if they’d have to lie low.”

“I’d forgotten Kay’s for the moment. Now you mention it, they are rather a crew. But I shouldn’t think they’d find it worth while to rot about here. It isn’t as if they were on their native heath. People have a prejudice against having their tent-ropes loosed, and they’d get beans if they did anything in that line. I remember once there was a tent which made itself objectionable, and it got raided in the night by a sort of vigilance committee from the other schools, and the chaps in it got the dickens of a time. None of them ever came to camp again. I hope Kay’s’ll try and behave decently. It’ll be an effort for them; but I hope they’ll make it. It would be an awful nuisance if young Billy made an ass of himself in any way. He loves making an ass of himself. It’s a sort of hobby of his.”

===Chinese===
肯尼迪注视着他们，触景生思，便在熄灯前两人进帐篷时问吉米·西尔弗，军营里捣乱的事多不多。

「不算多，」这位行家说，「到一天结束，人一般都累瘫了，除了睡觉什么也不想干。不过，也有过先例。偶尔会有一顶帐篷去抄另一顶。把人家帐篷的绳子解开，你懂的。我认为是下三滥的勾当。干的人不多，一旦被查出来，收拾起来也是毫不留情。不过问这个干什么？你手痒了？」

「我只是忽然想到，这回凯氏来了一伙活宝。我在想他们有没有捣乱的机会，还是只好夹起尾巴。」

「我一时把凯氏给忘了。你这一提，他们还真是阵容可观。可我看他们犯不上在这儿撒野。这里不比他们的自家地盘。众人对帐篷绳子被解有着天然的偏见，他们要在这一行里有什么动作，准得吃不了兜着走。我记得有一回，有一顶帐篷惹了众怒，夜里被别校组成的一支义勇队抄了营，帐篷里那几位吃足了苦头。后来谁也没敢再来军营。我希望凯氏这回识相点，规规矩矩。这对他们是个考验，但我希望他们能挺住。小比利要是出什么洋相，那可烦死人了。他就爱出洋相。算是他的一项业余爱好。」

===Original===
As if to support the statement, a sudden volley of subdued shouts came from the other end of the Eckleton lines.

“Go it, Wren!”

“Stick to it, Silver!”

“Wren!”

“Silver!”

“S-s-h!”

Silence, followed almost immediately by a gruff voice inquiring with simple directness what the dickens all this noise was about.

===Chinese===
仿佛要给这句话作证似的，埃克尔顿营列那一头忽然传来一阵压低嗓门的齐声呐喊。

「加油，雷恩（Wren）！」

「顶住，西尔弗！」

「雷恩！」

「西尔弗！」

「嘘——！」

一阵安静，紧接着一个粗嗓子直截了当地发问：这吵吵嚷嚷的到底见什么鬼。

===Original===
“Hullo!” said Kennedy. “Did you hear that? I wonder what’s been up? Your brother was in it, whatever it was.”

“Of course,” said Jimmy Silver, “he would be. We can’t find out about it now, though. I’ll ask him tomorrow, if I remember. I shan’t remember, of course. Good night.”

“Good night.”

Half an hour later, Kennedy, who had been ruminating over the incident in his usual painstaking way, reopened the debate.

“Who’s Wren?” he asked.

“Wha’?” murmured Silver, sleepily.

“Who’s Wren?” repeated Kennedy.

“I d’know.﻿ ﻿… Oh.﻿ ﻿… Li’l’ beast.﻿ ﻿… Kay’s.﻿ ﻿… Red hair.﻿ ﻿… G’-ni’.”

And sleep reigned in Blackburn’s tent.

===Chinese===
「喂！」肯尼迪说，「听见没有？出什么事了？不管是什么，你弟弟反正有一份。」

「那当然，」吉米·西尔弗说，「他必有一份。不过现在也问不出所以然。明天我问他，要是记得起来的话。当然是记不起来的。晚安。」

「晚安。」

半小时后，肯尼迪照他一贯的钻研路数，把这件事翻来覆去咂摸了一阵，重新开了一场辩论。

「雷恩是谁？」他问。

「啥？」西尔弗睡意朦胧地咕哝。

「雷恩是谁？」肯尼迪又问。

「不知道。……哦。……小坏蛋。……凯氏的。……红头发。……晚——安。」

于是睡眠在布莱克本宿舍的帐篷里君临天下。

## VI The Raid on the Guard Tent / 第六章 袭击卫兵帐篷

===Original===
Wren and Billy Silver had fallen out over a question of space. It was Silver’s opinion that Wren’s nest ought to have been built a foot or two further to the left. He stated baldly that he had not room to breathe, and requested the redheaded one to ease off a point or so in the direction of his next-door neighbour. Wren had refused, and, after a few moments’ chatty conversation, smote William earnestly in the wind. Trouble had begun upon the instant. It had ceased almost as rapidly owing to interruptions from without, but the truce had been merely temporary. They continued the argument outside the tent at five-thirty the next morning, after the reveille had sounded, amidst shouts of approval from various shivering mortals who were tubbing preparatory to embarking on the labours of the day.

A brisk first round had just come to a conclusion when Walton lounged out of the tent, yawning.

Walton proceeded to separate the combatants. After which he rebuked Billy Silver with a swagger stick. Wren’s share in the business he overlooked. He was by way of being a patron of Wren’s, and he disliked Billy Silver, partly for his own sake and partly because he hated his brother, with whom he had come into contact once or twice during his career at Eckleton, always with unsatisfactory results.

So Walton dropped on to Billy Silver, and Wren continued his toilet rejoicing.

===Chinese===
雷恩和比利·西尔弗为了地盘问题翻了脸。西尔弗的意见是，雷恩的窝本该往左再挪一两英尺。他直截了当地声明自己连喘气的余地都没有，并请求这位红毛往隔壁邻居的方向让上一两分。雷恩不肯，几句亲切友好的交谈之后，便朝威廉的心窝结结实实捶了一拳。纠纷当场爆发。又因外人干涉，几乎同样迅速地平息，但停战纯属临时性质。第二天早晨五点半，起床号响过之后，两人到帐篷外接着理论，赢得一圈正打寒战、以盥洗预备开始一天劳苦的凡人的喝彩。

第一回合激战方酣之际，沃尔顿打着呵欠从帐篷里晃了出来。

沃尔顿上前拆开了交战双方。随后，他用一根短杖训诫了比利·西尔弗。雷恩在这桩公案里的责任，他一笔勾销。他勉强算是雷恩的靠山；而比利·西尔弗他不待见——一半是冲这小子本人，一半是恨他哥哥。在埃克尔顿的岁月里，他同那位哥哥打过一两回交道，回回都没讨着好。

于是沃尔顿扑向了比利·西尔弗，雷恩则欢欢喜喜继续梳洗。

===Original===
Camp was beginning the strenuous life now.
